**Maharashtra**[^cite_note-20] is a [state](375986.md) in the [western](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_India) peninsular region of [India](14533.md) occupying a substantial portion of the [Deccan Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau). It is bordered by the [Arabian Sea](2747.md) to the west, the Indian states of [Karnataka](16880.md) and [Goa](40010153.md) to the south, [Telangana](990267.md) to the southeast and [Chhattisgarh](47734.md) to the east, [Gujarat](53707.md) and [Madhya Pradesh](47945.md) to the north, and the Indian [union territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_territory) of [Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dadra_and_Nagar_Haveli_and_Daman_and_Diu) to the northwest.[^cite_note-21] Maharashtra is the [second-most populous state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_union_territories_of_India_by_population) in India, the third-most populous country subdivision in [South Asia](21566765.md), and the [fourth-most populous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_first-level_administrative_divisions_by_population) in the world.

The region that encompasses the modern state has a history going back many millennia. Notable dynasties that ruled the region include the [Asmakas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asmaka), the [Mauryas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurya), the [Satavahanas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satavahana_dynasty), the [Western Satraps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Satraps), the [Abhiras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abhira_dynasty), the [Vakatakas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vakataka_dynasty), the [Chalukyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chalukya_dynasty), the [Rashtrakutas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashtrakutas), the [Western Chalukyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Chalukya_Empire), the [Seuna Yadavas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seuna_\(Yadava\)_dynasty), the [Khaljis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalji_dynasty), the [Tughlaqs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tughlaq_dynasty), the [Bahamanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahmani_Sultanate) and the [Mughals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire). In the early 19th century, the region was divided between the Dominions of the [Peshwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peshwa) in the [Maratha Confederacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha_Confederacy) and the [Nizamate of Hyderabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizamate_of_Hyderabad).

Early in the 19th century, the British [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company) defeated the Marathas and brought most of present-day Maharashtra under its control, both directly and indirectly through many princely states. After 1858, the British crown assumed control of the area from the company. During the periods of Company and British rule, the region was divided between the [Bombay Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Presidency), the [Central Provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Provinces), [Berar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berar_Province), and many princely states including [Hyderabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyderabad_State).

After Indian independence in 1947, the Bombay Presidency became [Bombay State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_State) in the Indian Union. Between 1950 and 1956, Berar, the Deccan states and the Gujarat states were annexed into Bombay State. Aspirations of a separate state for [Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_language)-speaking peoples were pursued by the [United Maharashtra Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samyukta_Maharashtra_Samiti); their advocacy eventually borne fruit on 1 May 1960, when the State of Bombay was bifurcated into the modern states of Maharasthra and [Gujarat](53707.md).

The state is divided into 6 [divisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divisions_of_Maharashtra) and 36 [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_districts_of_Maharashtra). [Mumbai](19189.md) is the capital of Maharashtra and the [most populous urban area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_million-plus_urban_agglomerations_in_India) in India, with [Nagpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagpur) serving as the winter capital.[^cite_note-22] The [Godavari](47988.md) and [Krishna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishna_River) are the state's two major rivers, and forests cover 16.47% of the state's geographical area.

The [economy of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Maharashtra) is the [largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_GDP) in India, with a gross state domestic product (GSDP) of [₹](490783.md)42.5 trillion (US\$440 billion) and GSDP per capita of ₹335,247 (US\$3,500);[^cite_note-EcoSur21-23] it is the single-largest contributor to [India's economy](1472206.md), being accountable for 14% of all-India nominal [GDP](12594.md).[^cite_note-Maharashtra_data-24][^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26] The service sector dominates the state's economy, accounting for 69.3% of the value of the output of the country. Although agriculture accounts for 12% of the state GDP, it employs nearly half the population of the state.

Maharashtra is one of the most industrialised states in India. It includes 395 municipal councils[^cite_note-27] and 29 municipal corporations[^cite_note-28] which are urban *(cities)* local bodies in the state. The state capital, [Mumbai](19189.md), is India's [financial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_centre) and commercial capital.[^cite_note-Maharashtra_PC-29] The [Bombay Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Stock_Exchange), India's largest stock exchange and the oldest in Asia, is located in the city, as is the [National Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Stock_Exchange_of_India), which is the second-largest stock exchange in India and one of world's largest [derivatives exchanges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derivatives_exchange). The state has played a significant role in the country's [social](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_India) and [political life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_India) and is widely considered a leader in terms of [agricultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_productivity) and [industrial production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_production), trade and transport, and education.[^cite_note-Maharashtra_info-30] Maharashtra is the [ninth-highest ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_Human_Development_Index) among Indian states in the [human development index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_development_index).[^cite_note-31]

The state is home to seven [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](44940.md): [Ajanta Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajanta_Caves), [Ellora Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellora_Caves), [Elephanta Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elephanta_Caves), [Chhatrapati Shivaji Terminus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhatrapati_Shivaji_Terminus) (formerly Victoria Terminus), the [Victorian Gothic and Art Deco Ensembles of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victorian_Gothic_and_Art_Deco_Ensembles_of_Mumbai), the [Maratha Military Landscapes of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha_Military_Landscapes_of_India) (shared with Tamil Nadu) and the [Western Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Ghats), a heritage site made up of 39 individual properties, of which four are located in Maharashtra.[^cite_note-32][^cite_note-33]

## Etymology

See also: [List of Indian state and union territory name etymologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_state_and_union_territory_name_etymologies)

Pre-independence era writers state that the term "Maharashtra" was derived from "the country of [Mahars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahars)". Irawati Karve also noted that Mahars were found in all regions of Maharashtra.[^cite_note-34] The modern [Marathi language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_language) evolved from [Maharashtri Prakrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtri_Prakrit),[^cite_note-The_Linguist_List-35] and the word *[Marhatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marhatta_\(region\))* (later used for the [Marathas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha)) is found in the [Jain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jain) Maharashtrian literature. The term Maharashtra along with Maharashtrian, Marathi, and Maratha may have derived from the same root. However, their exact etymology is uncertain.[^cite_note-Gazetteer1967-36]

Another theory among the linguistic scholars is that the words Maratha and Maharashtra ultimately derived from a combination of *Mahā* and *Rāṣṭrikā*,[^cite_note-Gazetteer1967-36][^cite_note-auto1-37] the name of a tribe or dynasty of chiefs ruling in the [Deccan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau) region.[^cite_note-KBala1965-38] An alternate theory states that the term is derived from *mahā* ("great") and *[ratha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratha)*/*rathi* ("[chariot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chariot)"/"charioteer"), which refers to a skilful northern fighting force that migrated southward into the area.[^cite_note-KBala1965-38][^cite_note-auto1-37]

In the [Harivamsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harivamsa), the [Yadava kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yadava) called [Anaratta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarta_Kingdom) is described as mostly inhabited by the [Abhiras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abhira_people) (Abhira-praya-manusyam). The Anartta country and its inhabitants were called [Surastra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saurashtra_\(region\)) and the Saurastras, probably after the Rattas (Rastras) akin to the Rastrikas of Asoka's rock Edicts, now known as Maharastra and the [Marattas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_people).[^cite_note-39]

An alternative theory states that the term derives from the word *mahā* ("great") and *rāṣṭra* ("nation/dominion").[^cite_note-40] However, this theory is somewhat controversial among modern scholars who believe it to be the Sanskritised interpretation of later writers.[^cite_note-Gazetteer1967-36]

## History

Main articles: [History of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Maharashtra), [Maratha Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha_Empire), and [Samyukta Maharashtra Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samyukta_Maharashtra_Movement)

See also: [Chronology of statehood of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chronology_of_statehood_of_Maharashtra)

Numerous [Late Harappan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Harappan) or [Chalcolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chalcolithic) sites belonging to the [Jorwe culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorwe_culture) (c. 1300–700 BCE) have been discovered throughout the state.[^cite_note-41][^cite_note-42] The largest settlement discovered of the culture is at [Daimabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daimabad), which had a mud fortification during this period, as well as an elliptical temple with fire pits.[^cite_note-Lahiri2015-43][^cite_note-singh-44] In the [Late Harappan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Harappan) period there was a large migration of people from Gujarat to northern Maharashtra.[^cite_note-45]

Maharashtra was ruled by [Maurya Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurya_Empire) in the fourth and third centuries BCE. Around 230 BCE, Maharashtra came under the rule of the [Satavahana dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satavahana_dynasty) which ruled it for the next 400 years.[^cite_note-autogenerated440-46] The rule of Satavahanas was followed by that of [Western Satraps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Satraps), [Gupta Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gupta_Empire), [Gurjara-Pratihara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gurjara-Pratihara), [Vakataka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vakataka), [Kadambas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadambas), [Chalukya Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chalukya_Empire), [Rashtrakuta Dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashtrakuta_Dynasty), and [Western Chalukya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Chalukya) and the [Yadava dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yadava_dynasty). The [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist) [Ajanta Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajanta_Caves) in present-day [Aurangabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurangabad_district,_Maharashtra) display influences from the Satavahana and Vakataka styles. The caves were possibly excavated during this period.[^cite_note-Javid2008-47]

The Chalukya dynasty ruled the region from the sixth to the eighth centuries CE, and the two prominent rulers were [Pulakeshin II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulakeshin_II), who defeated the north Indian Emperor [Harsha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harsha), and [Vikramaditya II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vikramaditya_II), who defeated the [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab) invaders in the eighth century. The Rashtrakuta dynasty ruled Maharashtra from the eighth to the tenth century.[^cite_note-Indian_History_–_page_B-57-48] The Arab traveller [Sulaiman al Mahri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulaiman_al_Mahri) described the ruler of the Rashtrakuta dynasty [Amoghavarsha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amoghavarsha) as "one of the four great kings of the world".[^cite_note-autogenerated203-49] Shilahara dynasty began as vassals of the Rashtrakuta dynasty which ruled the Deccan plateau between the eighth and tenth centuries. From the early 11th century to the 12th century, the Deccan Plateau, which includes a significant part of Maharashtra, was dominated by the [Western Chalukya Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Chalukya_Empire) and the [Chola dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chola_dynasty).[^cite_note-autogenerated365-50] Several battles were fought between the Western Chalukya Empire and the Chola dynasty in the Deccan Plateau during the reigns of [Raja Raja Chola I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raja_Raja_Chola_I), [Rajendra Chola I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajendra_Chola_I), [Jayasimha II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayasimha_II_\(Western_Chalukya_dynasty\)), [Someshvara I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Someshvara_I), and [Vikramaditya VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vikramaditya_VI).[^cite_note-51]

In the early 14th century, the [Yadava Dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yadava_Dynasty), which ruled most of present-day Maharashtra, was overthrown by the [Delhi Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sultanate) ruler [Alauddin Khalji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alauddin_Khalji). Later, [Muhammad bin Tughluq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_bin_Tughluq) conquered parts of the Deccan, and temporarily shifted his capital from Delhi to [Daulatabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daulatabad,_Maharashtra) in Maharashtra. After the collapse of the Tughluqs in 1347, the local [Bahmani Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahmani_Sultanate) of Gulbarga took over, governing the region for the next 150 years.[^cite_note-52] After the break-up of the [Bahamani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahamani) sultanate in 1518, Maharashtra split into five [Deccan Sultanates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Sultanates): [Nizamshah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmadnagar_Sultanate) of [Ahmednagar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmadnagar_Sultanate), [Adilshah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adilshah) of [Bijapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bijapur), [Qutubshah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qutubshah) of [Golkonda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golkonda), [Bidarshah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bidarshah) of [Bidar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bidar) and [Imadshah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imadshah) of Elichpur. These kingdoms often fought with each other. United, they decisively defeated the [Vijayanagara Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayanagara_Empire) of the south in 1565.[^cite_note-Saletore1934-53] The present area of Mumbai was ruled by the [Sultanate of Gujarat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Gujarat) before its capture by [Portugal](23033.md) in 1535 and the [Faruqi dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faruqi_dynasty) ruled the [Khandesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khandesh) region between 1382 and 1601 before finally getting annexed in the [Mughal Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire). [Malik Ambar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malik_Ambar), the regent of the [Nizamshahi dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmadnagar_Sultanate) of [Ahmednagar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmednagar) from 1607 to 1626,[^cite_note-54] increased the strength and power of [Murtaza Nizam Shah II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murtaza_Nizam_Shah_II) and raised a large army.Ambar is said to have introduced the concept of [guerrilla warfare](12720.md) in the Deccan region.[^cite_note-55] Malik Ambar assisted Mughal emperor [Shah Jahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Jahan) in Delhi against his stepmother, [Nur Jahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nur_Jahan), who wanted to enthrone her son-in-law.[^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57] Both [Shivaji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shivaji)'s grandfather, Maloji and father Shahaji served under Ambar.[^cite_note-58]

In the early 17th century, [Shahaji Bhosale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahaji_Bhosale), an ambitious local general who had served the [Ahmadnagar Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmadnagar_Sultanate), the Mughals and [Adil Shah of Bijapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adil_Shahi_dynasty) at different periods throughout his career, attempted to establish his independent rule.[^cite_note-59] This attempt was unsuccessful, but his son Shivaji succeeded in establishing the [Maratha Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha_Empire).[^cite_note-60] Shortly after Shivaji's death in 1680, the Mughal emperor Aurangzeb launched a campaign to conquer Maratha territories as well as the Adilshahi and Govalkonda kingdoms.[^cite_note-\:2-61] This campaign, better known as [Mughal–Maratha Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal%E2%80%93Maratha_Wars), was a strategic defeat for Mughals. Aurangzeb failed to fully conquer Maratha territories, and this campaign had a ruinous effect on Mughal Treasury and Army.[^cite_note-62] Shortly after Aurangzeb's death in 1707, Marathas under Peshwa [Bajirao I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bajirao_I) and the generals that he had promoted such as [Ranoji Shinde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranoji_Shinde) and [Malharrao Holkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malharrao_Holkar) started conquering Mughal Territories in the north and western India, and by 1750s they or their successors had confined the Mughals to city of Delhi.[^cite_note-63]

After their defeat at the hand of [Ahmad Shah Abdali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_Shah_Abdali)'s Afghan forces in the [Third Battle of Panipat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Battle_of_Panipat) in 1761, the Maratha suffered a setback. However, they soon reclaimed the lost territories and ruled central and north India including Delhi until the end of the eighteenth century. The Marathas also developed a potent *[Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha_Navy)* circa in the 1660s, which at its peak under the command of [Kanhoji Angre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanhoji_Angre), dominated the territorial waters of the western coast of India from Mumbai to [Savantwadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savantwadi).[^cite_note-64] It resisted the [British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Company_Rule_in_India), [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_India), [Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_India), and [Siddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siddi) naval ships and kept a check on their naval ambitions. Charles Metcalfe, British Civil servant and later Acting Governor-General, said in 1806:[^cite_note-65]

> India contains no more than two great powers, British and Maratha, and every other state acknowledges the influence of one or the other. Every inch that we recede will be occupied by them.

The British [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company) slowly expanded areas under its rule during the 18th century. The [Third Anglo-Maratha War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Anglo-Maratha_War) (1817–1818) led to the end of the Maratha Empire and the [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company) took over the empire.[^cite_note-66][^cite_note-Rathod1994-67] The [Maratha Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha_Navy) dominated till around the 1730s, was in a state of decline by the 1770s and ceased to exist by 1818.[^cite_note-68]

The [British](31717.md) governed western Maharashtra as part of the [Bombay Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Presidency), which spanned an area from [Karachi](17123.md) in Pakistan to northern Deccan. A number of the [Maratha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha) states persisted as [princely states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princely_state), retaining autonomy in return for acknowledging British [suzerainty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suzerainty). The largest princely states in the territory were [Nagpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagpur_kingdom), [Satara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satara_state) and [Kolhapur State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolhapur_State); Satara was annexed to the Bombay Presidency in 1848, and Nagpur was annexed in 1853 to become [Nagpur Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagpur_Province), later part of the [Central Provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Provinces). [Berar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berar_Province), which had been part of the [Nizam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizam) of [Hyderabad's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyderabad_State) kingdom, was occupied by the British in 1853 and annexed to the Central Provinces in 1903.[^cite_note-Russell1997-69] However, a large region called [Marathwada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathwada) remained part of the Nizam's Hyderabad State throughout the British period.

The British ruled the Maharashtra region from 1818 to 1947 and influenced every aspect of life for the people of the region. They brought several changes to the legal system,[^cite_note-70][^cite_note-71][^cite_note-72] built modern means of transport including roads[^cite_note-73] and railways,[^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75] took various steps to provide mass education, including that for previously marginalised classes and women,[^cite_note-76] established universities based on western system and imparting education in science, technology,[^cite_note-77] and western medicine,[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79][^cite_note-80] standardised the Marathi language,[^cite_note-81][^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83][^cite_note-Rao1994-84] and introduced mass media by utilising modern printing technologies.[^cite_note-85] The [1857 war of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Rebellion_of_1857) had many Marathi leaders, though the battles mainly took place in northern India. The modern struggle for independence started taking shape in the late 1800s with leaders such as [Bal Gangadhar Tilak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bal_Gangadhar_Tilak), Justice [Mahadev Govind Ranade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahadev_Govind_Ranade), [Gopal Krishna Gokhale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopal_Krishna_Gokhale), [Pherozeshah Mehta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pherozeshah_Mehta) and [Dadabhai Naoroji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dadabhai_Naoroji) evaluating the company rule and its consequences. [Jyotirao Phule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyotirao_Phule) was the pioneer of social reform in the Maharashtra region in the second half of the 19th century. [Shahu, Raja of Kolhapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahu_of_Kolhapur) and continued Phule's work, as well as [B. R. Ambedkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B._R._Ambedkar) later on. After the partial [autonomy](191145.md) given to the states by the [Government of India Act 1935](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_India_Act_1935), [B. G. Kher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B._G._Kher) became the first chief minister of the Congress party-led government of the trilingual Bombay Presidency.[^cite_note-86] The ultimatum to the British during the [Quit India Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quit_India_Movement) was given in Mumbai and culminated in the transfer of power and independence in 1947.

After Indian independence, princely states and Jagirs of the [Deccan States Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_States_Agency) were merged into [Bombay State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_State), which was created from the former Bombay Presidency in 1950.[^cite_note-87] In 1956, the [States Reorganisation Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_Reorganisation_Act) reorganised the Indian states along linguistic lines, and Bombay Presidency State was enlarged by the addition of the predominantly [Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_language)-speaking regions of [Marathwada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathwada) ([Aurangabad Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurangabad_Division)) from erstwhile [Hyderabad state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyderabad_state) and [Vidarbha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidarbha) region from the [Central Provinces and Berar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Provinces_and_Berar). The southernmost part of Bombay State was ceded to [Mysore](16880.md). In the 1950s, Marathi people strongly protested against bilingual [Bombay state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_state) under the banner of [Samyukta Maharashtra Samiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samyukta_Maharashtra_Samiti).[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-89] The notable leaders of the samiti included [Keshavrao Jedhe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keshavrao_Jedhe), [S.M. Joshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S.M._Joshi), [Shripad Amrit Dange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shripad_Amrit_Dange), [Pralhad Keshav Atre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pralhad_Keshav_Atre) and [Gopalrao Khedkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopalrao_Khedkar). The key demand of the samiti called for a Marathi speaking state with Mumbai as its capital.[^cite_note-90] In the Gujarati speaking areas of the state, a similar [Mahagujarat Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahagujarat_Movement) demanded a separate Gujarat state comprising majority Gujarati areas. After many years of protests, which saw 106 deaths among the protestors, and electoral success of the samiti in 1957 elections, the central government led by Prime minister [Nehru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nehru) split Bombay State into two new states of Maharashtra and Gujarat on 1 May 1960.[^cite_note-91]

The state continues to have a dispute with Karnataka regarding the region of [Belgaum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgaum) and [Karwar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karwar).[^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93] The Government of Maharashtra was unhappy with the border demarcation of 1957 and filed a petition to the Ministry of Home Affairs of India.[^cite_note-94] Maharashtra claimed 814 villages, and 3 urban settlements of Belagon, Karwar and [Nippani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nipani), all part of the then Bombay Presidency.[^cite_note-95] A petition by Maharashtra in the Supreme Court of India, staking a claim over Belagon, is currently pending.[^cite_note-96]

Further information: [Belagavi border dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belagavi_border_dispute)

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Maharashtra)



[ Bramhagiri hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brahmagiri_\(Maharashtra\)) in Sahyadri mountain range ([Western Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Ghats))



[Wainganga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wainganga) River near Bhandara district

Maharashtra with a total area of 307,713 km<sup>2</sup> (118,809 sq mi), is the [third-largest state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_territories_of_India_by_area) by area in terms of land area and constitutes 9.36% of India's total geographical area. The State lies between 15°35' N to 22°02' N latitude and 72°36' E to 80°54' E longitude. It occupies the [western](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_India) and central part of the country and has a coastline stretching 840 kilometres (520 mi)[^cite_note-97] along the [Arabian Sea](2747.md).[^cite_note-outline-98] The dominant physical feature of the state is its plateau character, which is separated from the Konkan coastline by the mountain range of the Western Ghats, which runs parallel to the coast from north to south. The [Western Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Ghats), also known as the Sahyadri Range, has an average elevation of 1,200 metres (3,900 ft); its slopes gently descending towards the east and southeast.[^cite_note-western_ghat-99] The [Western Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Ghats) (or the Sahyadri Mountain range) provide a physical barrier to the state on the west, while the [Satpura Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satpura_Hills) along the north and Bhamragad-Chiroli-Gaikhuri ranges on the east serve as its natural borders.[^cite_note-farmer-100] This state's expansion from North to South is 720 km (450 mi) and East to West is 800 km (500 mi). To the west of these hills lie the [Konkan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkan) coastal plains, 50–80 km (31–50 mi) in width. To the east of the Ghats lies the flat [Deccan Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau). The main rivers of the state are the [Krishna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishna_River), and its tributary, [Bhima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhima_River), the Godavari, and its main tributaries, [Manjara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manjira_River), and [Wardha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wardha_River)-[Wainganga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wainganga_River) and the [Tapi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tapi_River,_India), and its tributary [Purna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purna_River_\(tributary_of_Tapti\)).[^cite_note-outline-98][^cite_note-rivers-101] Maharashtra is divided into five geographic regions. Konkan is the western coastal region, between the Western Ghats and the sea.[^cite_note-state_divisions-102] [Khandesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khandesh) is the north region lying in the valley of the Tapti, [Purna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purna_River_\(tributary_of_Tapti\)) river.[^cite_note-rivers-101] [Nashik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashik), [Malegaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malegaon) [Jalgaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalgaon), [Dhule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhule) and [Bhusawal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhusawal) are the major cities of this region.[^cite_note-cities-103] [Desh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desh,_Maharashtra) is in the centre of the state.[^cite_note-Desh_region-104] [Marathwada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathwada), which was a part of the princely [state of Hyderabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Hyderabad) until 1956, is located in the southeastern part of the state.[^cite_note-outline-98][^cite_note-marathwada-105] Aurangabad and [Nanded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanded) are the main cities of the region.[^cite_note-city-106] [Vidarbha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidarbha) is the easternmost region of the state, formerly part of the Central Provinces and Berar.[^cite_note-107]

The state has limited area under irrigation, low natural fertility of soils, and large areas prone to recurrent drought. Due to this the agricultural productivity of Maharashtra is generally low as compared to the national averages of various crops. Maharashtra has been divided in to nine agro-climatic zones on the basis of annual rainfall soil types, vegetation and cropping pattern.[^cite_note-Agro_Zones-108]

Further information: [Manas Rural Development Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manas_Rural_Development_Institute)

### Climate



Dried up Godavari River at [Puntamba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puntamba), Ahmadnagar district, after a poor [monsoon](57630.md) season

Maharashtra experiences a [tropical wet and dry climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_wet_and_dry_climate) with hot, rainy, and cold weather seasons. Some areas more inland experience a hot [semi arid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi_arid) climate, due to a rain shadow effect caused by the Western Ghats.[^cite_note-IGARSS2017-109] The month of March marks the beginning of the summer and the temperature rises steadily until June. In the central plains, summer temperatures rise to between 40 and 45 °C (104 and 113 °F). May is usually the warmest and January the coldest month of the year. The winter season lasts until February with lower temperatures occurring in December and January. On the Deccan plateau that lies on eastern side of the Sahyadri mountains, the climate is drier, however, dew and hail often occur, depending on seasonal weather.[^cite_note-Seasonal-110]

The rainfall patterns in the state vary by the topography of different regions. The state can be divided into four meteorological regions, namely coastal Konkan, Western Maharashtra, Marathwada, and Vidarbha.[^cite_note-111] The [southwest monsoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southwest_monsoon) usually arrives in the last week of June and lasts till mid-September. Pre-monsoon showers begin towards the middle of June and post-monsoon rains occasionally occur in October. The highest average monthly rainfall is during July and August. In the winter season, there may be a little rainfall associated with western winds over the region. The Konkan coastal area, west of the Sahyadri Mountains receives very heavy monsoon rains with an annual average of more than 3,000 millimetres (120 in). However, just 150 km (90 mi) to the east, in the rain shadow of the mountain range, only 500–700 mm (20–28 in) per year will fall, and long dry spells leading to drought are a common occurrence. Maharashtra has many of the 99 Indian districts identified by the Indian Central water commission as prone to drought.[^cite_note-Mall-112] The average annual rainfall in the state is 1,181 mm (46+1⁄2 in) and 75% of it is received during the southwest monsoon from June–to September. However, under the influence of the Bay of Bengal, eastern Vidarbha receives good rainfall in July, August, and September.[^cite_note-climate-113] Thane, [Raigad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raigad_district), [Ratnagiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratnagiri_district), and [Sindhudurg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sindhudurg_district) districts receive heavy rains of an average of 2,000 to 2,500 mm (80 to 100 in) and the hill stations of [Matheran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matheran) and [Mahabaleshwar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabaleshwar) over 5,000 mm (200 in). Contrariwise, the rain shadow districts of [Nashik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashik_district), [Pune](3123898.md), [Ahmednagar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmednagar_district), [Dhule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhule_district), [Jalgaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalgaon_district), [Satara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satara_district), [Sangli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangli_district), [Solapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solapur_district), and parts of [Kolhapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolhapur_district) receive less than 1,000 mm (39 in) annually. In winter, a cool dry spell occurs, with clear skies, gentle air breeze, and pleasant weather that prevails from October to February, although the eastern Vidarbha region receives rainfall from the north-east monsoon.[^cite_note-114]

## Flora and fauna



A Bengal tiger in [Tadoba Andhari Tiger Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tadoba_Andhari_Tiger_Reserve)

The state has three crucial biogeographic zones, namely [Western Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Ghats), [Deccan Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau), and the [West coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Coastal_Plains). The Ghats nurture endemic species, the Deccan Plateau provides for vast mountain ranges and grasslands while the coast is home to littoral and swamp forests.The flora of Maharashtra is heterogeneous in composition. In 2012, the recorded thick forest area in the state was 61,939 km<sup>2</sup> (23,915 sq mi) which was about 20.13% of the state's geographical area.[^cite_note-forest_percentile-115]

There are three main Public Forestry Institutions (PFIs) in the Maharashtra state: the [Maharashtra Forest Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Forest_Department) (MFD), the Forest Development Corporation of Maharashtra (FDCM) and the Directorate of Social Forestry (SFD).[^cite_note-Forestry-116] The Maharashtra State Biodiversity Board, constituted by the Government of Maharashtra in January 2012 under the Biological Diversity Act, 2002, is the nodal body for the conservation of biodiversity within and outside forest areas in the State.[^cite_note-117][^cite_note-118]

Maharashtra is ranked second among the [Indian states](375986.md) in terms of the recorded forest area. Recorded Forest Area (RFA) in the state is 159,489 km<sup>2</sup> (61,579 sq mi), of which 128,324 km<sup>2</sup> (49,546 sq mi) is reserved forests, 17,438 km<sup>2</sup> (6,733 sq mi) is protected forest and 13,727 km<sup>2</sup> (5,300 sq mi) is unclassed forests. Based on the interpretation of IRS [Resourcesat-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resourcesat-2) LISS III satellite data of the period Oct 2017 to Jan 2018, the State has 22,586 km<sup>2</sup> (8,720.53 sq mi) under Very Dense Forest (VDF), 53,282 km<sup>2</sup> (20,572.35 sq mi) under Moderately Dense Forest (MDF) and 55,645 km<sup>2</sup> (21,484.68 sq mi) under Open Forest (OF). According to the [Champion and Seth classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_George_Champion), Maharashtra has five types of forests:[^cite_note-119]

- Southern Tropical Semi-Evergreen forests: These are found in the western ghats at a height of 400–1,000 m (1,300–3,300 ft). [Anjani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memecylon_umbellatum), [Hirda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminalia_chebula), [Kinjal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminalia_paniculata), and [Mango](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mango) are predominant tree species found here.

- Southern Tropical Moist Deciduous forests: These are a mix of Moist [Teak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teak) bearing forests ([Melghat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melghat)) and Moist Mixed deciduous forests (Vidarbha and Thane district). Commercially important Teak, [Shishum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalbergia_sissoo), and [bamboo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bambusa_tulda) are found here. In addition to evergreen Teak, some of the other tree species found in this type of forest include [Jambul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jambul), [Ain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ain), and [Shisam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalbergia_latifolia).

  [^cite_note-wildlife-120]

- Southern Tropical Dry Deciduous forests: these occupy a major part of the state. Southern Tropical Thorn forests are found in the low rainfall regions of [Marathwada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathwada), [Vidarbha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidarbha), [Khandesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khandesh), and Western Maharashtra. At present, these forests are heavily degraded. [Babul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babul_\(tree\)), [Bor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ziziphus_mauritiana), and [Palas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butea_monosperma) are some of the tree species found here.

- [Littoral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Littoral_zone) and [Swamp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swamp_forest) forests are mainly found in the Creeks of [Sindhudurg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sindhudurg) and Thane districts of the coastal Konkan region. The state harbours significant mangrove, coastal and marine biodiversity, with 304 km

  2

  &#x20;(117 sq mi) of the area under mangrove cover as per the India State of Forest Report (ISFR) of the Forest survey India in the coastal districts of the state.

The most common animal species present in the state are monkeys, wild pigs, [tiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger), [leopard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopard), [gaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaur), [sloth bear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sloth_bear), [sambar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sambar_\(deer\)), [four-horned antelope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackbuck), [chital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chital), [barking deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barking_deer), [mouse deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mouse_deer), [small Indian civet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_Indian_civet), [golden jackal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_jackal), [jungle cat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jungle_cat), and [hare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hare).[^cite_note-fauna-121] Other animals found in this state include reptiles such as [lizards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lizard), scorpions and snake species such as [cobras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cobra) and [kraits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bungarus).[^cite_note-biodiversity-122] The state provides legal protection to its [tiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger) population through six dedicated [tiger reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_reserves_of_India) under the precincts of the [National Tiger Conservation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Tiger_Conservation_Authority).

The state's 720 km (450 mi) of sea coastline of the [Arabian Sea](2747.md) marks the presence of various types of fish and marine animals. The [Zoological Survey of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoological_Survey_of_India) (ZSI) found 1527 marine animal species, including 581 species of [molluscs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mollusca), many [crustacean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crustacean) species including crabs, shrimps, and lobsters, 289 fish species, and 141 species types of marine [annelids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annelid) (sea worms).[^cite_note-123]

## Regions, divisions and districts

Main article: [List of districts of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_districts_of_Maharashtra)

See also: [List of talukas of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_talukas_of_Maharashtra) and [List of cities in Maharashtra by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Maharashtra_by_population)

refer caption

Divisions of Maharashtra, along with their respective districts (with Palghar district formed in 2014 from the northern part of Thane district)

Maharashtra has the following geographical regions:

1. [North Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Maharashtra)

2. [Konkan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkan)

3. [Marathwada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathwada)

4. [Vidarbha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidarbha)

5. [Desh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desh,_Maharashtra) or Western Maharashtra

It consists of six administrative divisions:[^cite_note-124]

1. [Amravati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amravati_division)

2. [Aurangabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurangabad_division)

3. [Konkan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkan_division)

4. [Nagpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagpur_division)

5. [Nashik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashik_division)

6. [Pune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pune_division)

The state's six divisions are divided into 36 [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_districts_of_Maharashtra), 109 [sub-divisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subdivision_in_India), 358 [talukas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talukas_of_Maharashtra), and 44778 [revenue villages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revenue_Village).[^cite_note-talukas-125][^cite_note-126] The following map shows the 36 districts of Maharashtra.

Each district is administered by a [district collector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_collector) & district magistrate, appointed by the government either from the cadre of [Indian Administrative Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Administrative_Service) or the Maharashtra Civil Service.[^cite_note-civil-127] The Collector is assisted by additional collectors and deputy collectors. Districts are subdivided into *sub-divisions* (upavibhāga) administered by [sub-divisional magistrates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-divisional_magistrate) (*sub divisional officers*), and again into *talukas*.[^cite_note-Panchayat-128] Each taluka (tehsil) is headed by a [tahsildar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehsildar).[^cite_note-panchayati_raj-129][^cite_note-panchayats-130]

Each taluka comprises a group of villages, placed under a Circle Officer, and is divided into individual villages under [Talathis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Village_accountant), who are responsible for land revenue-related matters. The above divisions are primarily intended for land revenue administration. The local governance structure is such that a [block](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_development_block) consists of panchayats (village councils).[^cite_note-panchayati_raj-129][^cite_note-panchayats-130] [Panchayat Samitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panchayat_samiti) are intermediate level panchayat between the [Zilla Parishad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Councils_of_India) (district councils) at the district level and [gram panchayat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gram_panchayat) (village councils) at the lower level.[^cite_note-Panchayat-128][^cite_note-zilla-131]

Of Maharashtra's population, 45.22% of people live in urban regions. 50.8 million people live in urban areas. There are 29 [Municipal Corporations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Corporations), 247 [Municipal Councils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_council_\(India\)), and 147 [Nagar panchayats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagar_panchayat) in Maharashtra for urban governance.[^cite_note-Municipal-132][^cite_note-133]&#x20;

## Demographics

### Population

| Year | Pop. | ±% |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1901 | 19,391,643 | — |
| 1911 | 21,474,523 | +10.7% |
| 1921 | 20,849,666 | −2.9% |
| 1931 | 23,959,300 | +14.9% |
| 1941 | 26,832,758 | +12.0% |
| 1951 | 32,002,564 | +19.3% |
| 1961 | 39,553,718 | +23.6% |
| 1971 | 50,412,235 | +27.5% |
| 1981 | 62,782,818 | +24.5% |
| 1991 | 78,937,187 | +25.7% |
| 2001 | 96,878,627 | +22.7% |
| 2011 | 112,374,333 | +16.0% |
| Source: [Census of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Census_of_India)[^cite_note-134] |  |  |

According to the provisional results of the 2011 national census, Maharashtra was at that time the richest state in India and the [second-most populous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_union_territories_of_India_by_population) state in India with a population of 112,374,333. Contributing to 9.28% of India's population, males and females are 58,243,056 and 54,131,277, respectively.[^cite_note-contribution-135] The total population growth in 2011 was 15.99%, while in the previous decade it was 22.57%.[^cite_note-statics-136][^cite_note-proportion-137] Since independence, the decadal growth rate of population has remained higher (except in the year 1971) than the national average. However, in the year 2011, it was found to be lower than the national average.[^cite_note-proportion-137] The 2011 census for the state found 55% of the population to be rural with 45% being urban-based.[^cite_note-138] The state also includes a substantial number of [migrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_migration) from other states of India.[^cite_note-139] [Uttar Pradesh](231623.md), Gujarat, and Karnataka account for the largest percentage of migrants to the Mumbai metropolitan area.[^cite_note-140]

### Social groups

Although, India has not conducted a caste-wise census since Independence, based on the British era census of 1931, it is estimated that the [Maratha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha_\(caste\)) and the [Maratha-kunbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunbi) numerically form the largest caste cluster with around 32% of the population.[^cite_note-141] Maharashtra has a large [Other Backward Class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Other_Backward_Class) (OBC) population constituting 41% of the population. Some main OBC castes of Maharashtra include [Bari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bari_\(caste\)), [Koli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koli_people), [Gurjar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gurjars_in_Maharashtra), [Yadav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yadav), and [Rajput](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajput). The 2011 census found [scheduled castes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scheduled_castes) and [scheduled tribes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scheduled_tribes) to account for 11.8% and 8.9% of the population, respectively.[^cite_note-142] The main scheduled tribes in Maharashtra are [Andh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andh_people), [Bhil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhil), [Barda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barda_\(tribe\)), [Dhanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhanka), [Gondi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gondi_people), [Khond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khond_people), [Kolam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolam_people), [Korku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korku_people), [Halpati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halpati), [Thakar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thakar_tribe), [Warli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warli), [Konkana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katkari_people) and [Halba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halba_people).[^cite_note-tribes-143]

### Language

#### Main languages

Languages of Maharashtra (2011)

[^cite_note-2011census-language-144]

1. [Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_language) (68.9%)

2. [Hindi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindi) (12.9%)

3. [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) (6.71%)

4. [Gujarati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarati_language) (2.11%)

5. [Bhili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhili_language) (2.00%)

6. [Khandeshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khandeshi_language) (1.44%)

7. [Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_language) (1.18%)

8. [Kannada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannada) (0.89%)

9. Others (3.86%)

[Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_language) is the official language, spoken by 68.9%. Although different regions have their own dialects.[^cite_note-auto-6][^cite_note-Marathi-145][^cite_note-speakers-146] Most people speak regional languages classified as dialects of Marathi in the census. [Powari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powari_language), Lodhi, and [Varhadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varhadi_dialect) are spoken in the Vidarbha region, and Dangi is spoken near the Maharashtra-Gujarat border. [Bhil languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhil_languages) are spoken by 2% of people throughout the northwest part of the state. [Khandeshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khandeshi_language) (locally known as Ahirani) is spoken in Khandesh region. [Hindi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindi) is spoken by 12.9%. In the Desh and [Marathwada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathwada) regions, [Dakhini Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccani_language) is widely spoken by 6.71%, although Dakhini speakers are usually bilingual in Marathi.[^cite_note-147] [Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_language) spoken by 1.18% and [Kannada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannada_language) by 0.89%. The Hindi dialects Lodhi and Powari are spoken in the border area of [Madhya Pradesh](47945.md), Maharashtra, and [Chhattisgarh](47734.md).

Marathi is the first language of a majority or plurality of the people in all districts of Maharashtra except [Nandurbar](1684248.md), where [Bhili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhili_language) is spoken by 45% of its population. The highest percentage of [Khandeshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khandeshi_language) speakers are [Dhule district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhule_district) (29%) and the highest percentage of [Gondi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gondi_language) speakers are in [Gadchiroli district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadchiroli_district) (24%).[^cite_note-2011census-language-144]

The highest percentages of first language Hindi speakers are in urban areas, especially Mumbai and its suburbs, where it is the mother tongue to over a quarter of the population. Other urban centers like Pune, Nagpur, and Aurangabad also have a high number of Hindi-speakers. Combined with [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu), the persianized register of [Hindustani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustani_language), speakers of Hindi-Urdu form nearly 20% of the Maharashtrian population.[^cite_note-2011census-language-144] [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) and another Hindustani dialect, [Dakhni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccani_language), are spoken primarily by the Muslim population of the state.[^cite_note-148]

#### Other languages

Other languages spoken in Maharashtra include Gujarati at 2.11%, [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language) at 0.74%, [Sindhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sindhi_language) at 0.64%, [Bengali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_language) at 0.39%, Konkani at 0.35% Konkani, and [Gujari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujari) at 0.05%.[^cite_note-2011census-language-144]

*Gujari* is mainly spoken in Khandesh regions, especially [Nandurbar](1684248.md), [Dhule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhule_district) and [Jalgaon districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalgaon_district). [Konkani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkani_language), and its [Malvani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malvani_language) dialect are spoken along the southern Konkan coast. Telugu and Kannada are spoken near the Telangana and Karnataka borders. Lambadi is spoken across eastern Marathwada and western Vidarbha. Gondi is spoken by smaller group in Vidarbha, mainly in the forests of Gadchiroli and near the Telangana border.[^cite_note-2011census-language-144]

The [Mumbai metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Metropolitan_Region) is home to migrants from all over India. In Mumbai, a wide range of languages are spoken, including Gujarati, Telugu, Tamil, Konkani, Kannada, Sindhi, Punjabi, Bengali, Tulu, and many more.[^cite_note-2011census-language-144]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Maharashtra)

[Khandoba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khandoba) temple of Jejuri, Pune, Jama Masjid Nagpur

Religion in Maharashtra (2011)

[^cite_note-Religion-149]

1. [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Maharashtra) (79.8%)

2. [Islam](6037917.md) (11.5%)

3. [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_Buddhists) (5.81%)

4. [Jainism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism_in_Maharashtra) (1.25%)

5. [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Maharashtra) (0.96%)

6. [Sikhism](27964.md) (0.20%)

7. Other (0.16%)

8. Not stated (0.25%)

According to the 2011 census, [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) was the principal religion in the state at 79.8% of the total population. [Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_India) constituted 11.5% of the total population. Maharashtra has the highest number of followers of [Buddhism](3267529.md) in India, accounting for 5.8% of Maharashtra's total population with 6,531,200 followers. [Marathi Buddhists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_Buddhists) account for 77.36% of all [Buddhists in India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Buddhism_in_India).[^cite_note-census2011-150] [Sikhs](27964.md), [Christians](5211.md), and [Jains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism) constituted 0.2%, 1%, and 1.2% of the Maharashtra population respectively.[^cite_note-Religion-149]

Maharashtra, and particularly the city of Mumbai, is home to two tiny religious communities. This includes 5000 [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_India), mainly belonging to the [Bene Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bene_Israel), and [Baghdadi Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baghdadi_Jews) communities.[^cite_note-151] [Parsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parsi) is the other community who follow [Zoroastrianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoroastrianism). The 2011 census recorded around 44,000 parsis in Maharashtra.[^cite_note-152]

### Sex ratio and literacy rate

The 2011 census reported the human sex ratio is 929 females per 1000 males, which were below the national average of 943. The density of Maharashtra was 365 inhabitants per km<sup>2</sup> which was lower than the national average of 382 per km<sup>2</sup>. Since 1921, the populations of Ratnagiri and Sindhudurg shrank by −4.96% and −2.30%, respectively, while the population of Thane grew by 35.9%, followed by Pune at 30.3%. The literacy rate is 83.2%, higher than the national rate at 74.04%.[^cite_note-elite-153] Of this, male literacy stood at 89.82% and female literacy 75.48%.[^cite_note-154]

## Governance and administration

Main article: [Government of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Maharashtra)

See also: [Politics of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Maharashtra) and [List of chief ministers of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_chief_ministers_of_Maharashtra)



[Mantralaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mantralaya,_Mumbai) or administrative headquarters of Maharashtra state government in [South Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Mumbai)

The state is governed through a [parliamentary system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westminster_system) of [representative democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Representative_democracy), a feature the state shares with other Indian states. Maharashtra is one of the six states in India where the state legislature is bicameral, comprising the [Vidhan Sabha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidhan_Sabha) (Legislative Assembly) and the [Vidhan Parishad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidhan_Parishad) (Legislative Council).[^cite_note-mla's-155] The legislature, the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly, consists of elected members and special office bearers such as the Speaker and Deputy Speaker, who are elected by the members. The [Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Legislative_Assembly) consists of 288 members who are elected for five-year terms unless the Assembly is dissolved before to the completion of the term. The [Legislative Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Legislative_Council) is a permanent body of 78 members with one-third (33 members) retiring every two years. Maharashtra is the second most important state in terms of political representation in the [Lok Sabha](275456.md), or the lower chamber of the Indian Parliament with 48 seats which is next only to [Uttar Pradesh](231623.md) which has the highest number of seats than any other Indian state with 80 seats.[^cite_note-156] Maharashtra also has 19 seats in the [Rajya Sabha](232210.md), or the upper chamber of the Indian Parliament.[^cite_note-members-157][^cite_note-list-158]

The [government of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Maharashtra) is a democratically elected body in India with the [Governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_governors_of_Maharashtra) as its constitutional head who is appointed by the [President of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_India) for a five-year term.[^cite_note-Governor-159] The leader of the party or coalition with a majority in the Legislative Assembly is appointed as the [chief minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_minister) by the governor, and the Council of Ministers are appointed by the governor on the advice of the chief minister.[^cite_note-chief_minister-160] The governor remains a ceremonial head of the state, while the chief minister and his council are responsible for day-to-day government functions. The [council of ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thackeray_ministry) consists of Cabinet Ministers and Ministers of State (MoS). The Secretariat headed by the [Chief Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_secretary_\(India\)) assists the council of ministers. The Chief Secretary is also the administrative head of the government. Each government department is headed by a Minister, who is assisted by an [Additional Chief Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Additional_Chief_Secretary) or a [Principal Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principal_secretary_\(India\)), who is usually an officer of the [Indian Administrative Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Administrative_Service), the Additional Chief Secretary/Principal Secretary serves as the administrative head of the department they are assigned to. Each department also has officers of the rank of Secretary, Special Secretary, Joint Secretary, etc. assisting the Minister and the Additional Chief Secretary/Principal Secretary.[^cite_note-\:3-161]

For purpose of administration, the state is divided into 6 divisions and 36 [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_districts_of_Maharashtra). [Divisional commissioner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divisional_Commissioner_\(India\)), an IAS officer is the head of administration at the divisional level. The administration in each district is headed by a [Collector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_collector) & [District Magistrate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Magistrate), who is an IAS officer and is assisted by several officers belonging to state services. Urban areas in the state are governed by [Municipal Corporations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Corporations_in_India), [Municipal Councils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_governance_in_India), [Nagar Panchayats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagar_Panchayat_\(Notified_Area_Council\)), and seven [Cantonment Boards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantonment_Board).[^cite_note-proportion-137][^cite_note-divisions-162][^cite_note-163] Rural areas are governed by Panchayati Raj institutions: [Zilla Parishads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_council_\(India\)) at the [district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_districts_in_India) level, [Panchayat Samitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panchayat_samiti) at the [block](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_development_block) level, and [Gram Panchayats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gram_panchayat) at the [village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Village#India) level.[^cite_note-164] The [Maharashtra Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Police) is headed by an IPS officer of the rank of [Director general of police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Director_general_of_police) (DGP).[^cite_note-165] A [Superintendent of Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superintendent_of_police_\(India\)) (SP), an IPS officer assisted by the officers of the Maharashtra Police Service, is entrusted with the responsibility of police administration, maintaining law and order, crime prevention, crime investigation and related issues in each district.[^cite_note-Government_of_Maharashtra-166] In urban areas, a [Commissioner of Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commissioner_of_Police_\(India\)) (CP) heads the police force.[^cite_note-Government_of_Maharashtra-166] The Divisional Forest Officer (DFO), an officer belonging to the [Indian Forest Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Forest_Service), manages the forests, environment, and wildlife of the district, assisted by the officers of Maharashtra Forest Service and Maharashtra Forest Subordinate Service.[^cite_note-167] For revenue administration, each district is divided into subdivisions, headed by a [Sub-Divisional Officer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-divisional_magistrate) (SDO). A subdivision comprises several tehsils (taluks), each headed by a [Tehsildar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehsildar). A tehsil is further divided into revenue circles under Circle Officers, and each circle consists of multiple revenue villages under Village Revenue Officers, who are responsible for land revenue–related matters.

Other development departments of the state government, such as Rural Development, Education, Health, Public Works, Transport are responsible for discharging their respective functions within each district. Each headed by their respective district heads.[^cite_note-\:3-161]

Maharashtra High Court

The [Bombay High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_High_Court), one of the oldest high courts in India

The judiciary in the state consists of the Maharashtra High Court ([The High Court of Bombay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_High_Court)), district and session courts in each district and lower courts and judges at the taluka level.[^cite_note-judiciary-168] The High Court has regional branches at [Nagpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagpur) and [Aurangabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurangabad) in Maharashtra and [Panaji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panaji) which is the capital of Goa.[^cite_note-bombayhighcourt-169] The state cabinet on 13 May 2015 passed a resolution favouring the setting up of one more bench of the Bombay high court in [Kolhapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolhapur), covering the region.[^cite_note-timesofindia-170] The President of India appoints the chief justice of the High Court of the Maharashtra judiciary on the advice of the chief justice of the Supreme Court of India as well as the Governor of Maharashtra.[^cite_note-171] Other judges are appointed by the chief justice of the high court of the judiciary on the advice of the Chief Justice.[^cite_note-jdistrict-172] Subordinate Judicial Service is another vital part of the judiciary of Maharashtra.[^cite_note-173] The subordinate judiciary or the district courts are categorised into two divisions: the Maharashtra civil judicial services and higher judicial service.[^cite_note-Introduction-174] While the Maharashtra civil judicial services comprises the Civil Judges (Junior Division)/Judicial Magistrates and civil judges (Senior Division)/Chief Judicial Magistrate, the higher judicial service comprises civil and sessions judges.[^cite_note-activities-175] The Subordinate judicial service of the judiciary is controlled by the District Judge.[^cite_note-jdistrict-172][^cite_note-176]

### Politics

See also: [Politics of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Maharashtra) and [List of chief ministers of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_chief_ministers_of_Maharashtra)

The politics of the state in the first decades after its formation in 1960 was dominated by the [Indian National Congress](149333.md) party or its offshoots such as the [Nationalist Congress Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Congress_Party). At present, it has been dominated by four political parties, the [Bharatiya Janata Party](149330.md), the [Nationalist Congress Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Congress_Party), the [Indian National Congress](149333.md) and Shinde's [Shiv Sena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiv_Sena_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)).The politics of the state in the last five years has seen long-term alliances breaking up like that of undivided Shivsena and BJP, new ones being formed between Congress, NCP, and the Shivsena, regional parties like the Shivsena and NCP splitting up, and majority of their legislators joining a new alliance government with the BJP.[^cite_note-karthikeyan-177][^cite_note-\:4-178][^cite_note-BBC_23_June-179][^cite_note-auto3-180]

Just like in other states in India, dynastic politics is fairly common also among political parties in Maharashtra.[^cite_note-Denyer2014-181] The dynastic phenomenon is seen from the national level down to the district level and even village level. The three-tier structure of Panchayati Raj created in the state in the 1960s also helped to create and consolidate this phenomenon in rural areas. Apart from controlling the government, political families also control cooperative institutions, mainly [cooperative sugar factories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooperative_sugar_factories_in_Maharashtra) and district [cooperative banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooperative_movement_in_India) in the state.[^cite_note-182] The [Bharatiya Janata Party](149330.md) also features several senior leaders who are dynasts.[^cite_note-183][^cite_note-Chandra2016-184] In Maharashtra, the [NCP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Congress_Party) has a particularly high level of dynasticism.[^cite_note-Chandra2016-184]

In the early years, the politics of Maharashtra was dominated by Congress party figures such as [Yashwantrao Chavan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yashwantrao_Chavan), [Vasantdada Patil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasantdada_Patil), [Vasantrao Naik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasantrao_Naik), and [Shankarrao Chavan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shankarrao_Chavan). [Sharad Pawar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharad_Pawar), who started his political career in the Congress party, has been a towering personality in state and national politics for over forty years. During his career, he has split the Congress twice with significant consequences for the state politics.[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186] The Congress party enjoyed a near unchallenged dominance of the political landscape until 1995 when the [Shiv Sena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiv_Sena_\(1966%E2%80%932022\)) and the [Bharatiya Janata Party](149330.md) (BJP) secured an overwhelming majority in the state to form a coalition government.[^cite_note-187] After his second parting from the Congress party in 1999, [Sharad Pawar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharad_Pawar) founded the [NCP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Congress_Party) but then formed a coalition with the Congress to keep out the BJP-Shiv Sena combine out of the Maharashtra state government for fifteen years until September 2014. [Prithviraj Chavan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prithviraj_Chavan) of the Congress party was the last [Chief Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Minister) of Maharashtra under the Congress-NCP alliance.[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190] For the [2014 assembly polls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Maharashtra_Legislative_Assembly_election), the two alliances between NCP and Congress and that between BJP and Shiv Sena respectively broke down over seat allocations. In the election, the largest number of seats went to the Bharatiya Janata Party, with 122 seats. The BJP initially formed a minority government under [Devendra Fadnavis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devendra_Fadnavis). The Shiv Sena entered the Government after two months and provided a comfortable majority for the alliance in the [Maharashtra Vidhansabha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Legislative_Assembly) for the duration of the assembly.[^cite_note-191] In the [2019 Lok Sabha elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Indian_general_election), the BJP-Shiv Sena alliance secured 41 seats out of 48 from the state.[^cite_note-192] Later in 2019, the BJP and Shiv Sena alliance fought the assembly elections together but the alliance broke down after the election over the post of the chief minister. [Uddhav Thackeray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uddhav_Thackeray) of Shiv Sena then formed an alternative governing coalition under his leadership with his erstwhile opponents from NCP, INC, and several independent members of the legislative assembly.[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194] Thackeray served as the 19th [Chief Minister of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_minister_of_Maharashtra) of the [Maha Vikas Aghadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maha_Vikas_Aghadi) coalition until June 2022.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-IE28Nov-196][^cite_note-FP29Nov-197]

In late June 2022, [Eknath Shinde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eknath_Shinde), a senior [Shiv Sena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiv_Sena_\(1966%E2%80%932022\)) leader, and the majority of [MLAs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_the_Legislative_Assembly) from Shiv Sena joined hands with the BJP.[^cite_note-karthikeyan-177][^cite_note-\:4-178][^cite_note-BBC_23_June-179] Governor [Bhagat Singh Koshyari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhagat_Singh_Koshyari) called for a trust vote, an action that would later on be described as a "sad spectacle" by [Supreme Court of India](395852.md),[^cite_note-198] and draw criticism from Political Observers.[^cite_note-199] [Uddhav Thackeray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uddhav_Thackeray) resigned from the post as chief minister well as a MLC member ahead of [no-confidence motion](208101.md) on 29 June 2022.[^cite_note-200] Shinde subsequently formed a new coalition with the BJP, and was sworn in as the Chief Minister on 30 June 2022.[^cite_note-\:0-201] BJP leader, [Devendra Fadnavis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devendra_Fadnavis) was given the post of [Deputy Chief Minister](21740331.md) in the new government.[^cite_note-\:0-201] [Uddhav Thackeray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uddhav_Thackeray) filed a lawsuit in [Supreme Court of India](395852.md) claiming that [Eknath Shinde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eknath_Shinde) and his group's actions meant that they were disqualified under [Anti-defection law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-defection_law_\(India\)), with [Eknath Shinde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eknath_Shinde) claiming that he has not defected, but rather represents the true Shiv Sena party.[^cite_note-\:1-202][^cite_note-article66628047-203] The [Supreme court](395852.md) delivered its verdict in May 2023. In its verdict the five-judge constitution bench of the Supreme court ruled that the Maharashtra governor and assembly speaker did not act as per the law.[^cite_note-204] However, the court said that it cannot order the restoration of the Uddhav Thackeray government as Thackeray resigned without facing a floor test.[^cite_note-205][^cite_note-\:1-202][^cite_note-article66628047-203] Supreme Court also asked the Assembly Speaker to decide on the matter of disqualification of 16 MLAs including Chief Minister Eknath Shinde.[^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207] The case for decision on which faction has rights to use Shiv Sena Name and Symbol is currently being heard by Supreme Court.[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209]

In July 2023, [NCP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Congress_Party) leader [Ajit Pawar](16590268.md), and a number of NCP state assembly members joined the Shivsena- BJP government led by Eknath Shinde.[^cite_note-auto3-180] Sharad Pawar, the founder of NCP, condemned the move and expelled the rebels. Ajit Pawar received support from majority of party legislators and office holders of the party.His faction was declared the official NCP and received the right to use the NCP election symbol of clock by the [Election Commission of India](47863083.md).[^cite_note-210]

In the [2024 Loksabha elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Indian_general_election_in_Maharashtra), the [INDIA grouping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_National_Developmental_Inclusive_Alliance) which includes the Mahavikas aghadi won a resounding victory with the majority of the state Loksabha seats, however in the Vidhan Sabha elections held on November 2024, the Mahayuti coalition of BJP, Shivsena (Shinde), and NCP (Ajit Pawar) winning 80% of the Vidhan sabha seats.[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212] In December 2024, Devendra Phadnavis became the chief minister of the Mahayuti government with Eknath Shinde, and Ajit Pawar being appointed as deputy chief ministers. Election analysts have credited the Ladki Bahin Yojana that made cash transfers to women before the election as attracting a larger number of female voters to opt for the Mahayuti. Other factors cited by analysts including greater participation by the RSS in ground level campaigning, continued popularity of Narendra Modi, and dog-whistle call for Hindu Unity using slogans such as «Ek Hai To Safe Hai» (If We're United, We are Strong and Safe against All Threats). Meanwhile, this slogan also appealed to the Muslims and a significant number of Muslim votes also returned to MahaYuti in Vidhan Sabha elections providing them a super strong mandate.[^cite_note-213]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Maharashtra)
