The economy of Maharashtra is driven by manufacturing, international trade, [mass media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media) (television, motion pictures, video games, recorded music), aerospace, technology, petroleum, fashion, apparel, and tourism.[^cite_note-India2007-215] Maharashtra is the most industrialised state and has maintained the leading position in the industrial sector in India.[^cite_note-strategies-216] The State is a pioneer in small scale industries.[^cite_note-217] Mumbai, the capital of the state and the [financial capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_capital) of India, houses the headquarters of most of the major corporate and financial institutions. India's main [stock exchanges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Stock_Exchange) and capital market and commodity exchanges are located in Mumbai. The state continues to attract industrial investments from domestic as well as foreign institutions. Maharashtra has the largest proportion of [taxpayers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxpayers) in India and its share markets transact almost 70% of the country's stocks.[^cite_note-218]

According to most recent data, Maharashtra is considered the richest state in India. As of 2024, Maharashtra is the richest state in India, with a projected GSDP of ₹42.67 lakh crore for the 2024-25 fiscal year.[^cite_note-219]

The [service sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_sector) dominates the economy of Maharashtra, accounting for 61.4% of the value addition and 69.3% of the value of output in the state.[^cite_note-service-220] The state's per-capita income in 2014 was 40% higher than the all-India average in the same year.[^cite_note-221] The gross state domestic product (GSDP) at current prices for 2021–22 is estimated at \$420 billion and contributes about 14.2% of the GDP. The agriculture and allied activities sector contributes 13.2% to the state's income. In 2012, Maharashtra reported a revenue surplus of ₹1524.9 million (US\$24 million), with total revenue of ₹1,367,117 million (US\$22 billion) and spending of ₹1,365,592.1 million (US\$22 billion).[^cite_note-service-220] Maharashtra is the largest FDI destination of India. FDI inflows in the State since April 2000 to September 2021 totalled ₹9,59,746 crore, which was 28.2% of total FDI inflows at the all-India level. With 11,308 startups, Maharashtra has the highest number of recognised startups in India.



Mumbai is a major contributor to the economy of Maharashtra.

Maharashtra contributes 25% of India's industrial output[^cite_note-wealth-222] and is the most indebted state in the country.[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224] Industrial activity in state is concentrated in Seven districts: [Mumbai City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_City_district), [Mumbai Suburban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Suburban_district), [Thane](638817.md), [Aurangabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurangabad_district,_Maharashtra), [Pune](3123898.md), [Nagpur](3356007.md), and [Nashik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashik_district).[^cite_note-investment-225] Mumbai has the largest share in GSDP (19.5%), both Thane and Pune districts contribute about same in the Industry sector, Pune district contributes more in the agriculture and allied activities sector, whereas Thane district contributes more in the Services sector.[^cite_note-investment-225] Nashik district shares highest in the agricultural and allied activities sector, but is behind in the Industry and Services sectors as compared to Thane and Pune districts.[^cite_note-investment-225]

Industries in Maharashtra include chemical and chemical products (17.6%), food and food products (16.1%), refined petroleum products (12.9%), machinery and equipment (8%), textiles (6.9%), basic metals (5.8%), motor vehicles (4.7%) and furniture (4.3%).[^cite_note-Service_sector-226] Maharashtra is the manufacturing hub for some of the largest public sector industries in India, including [Hindustan Petroleum Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustan_Petroleum_Corporation), [Tata Petrodyne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Group) and [Oil India Ltd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_India_Ltd).[^cite_note-227]

Maharashtra is the leading Indian state for many [Creative industries](674483.md) including [advertising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advertising), [architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture), [art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art), [crafts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crafts), [design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Design), [fashion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fashion), [film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_industry), [music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_industry), [performing arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Performing_arts), [publishing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Publishing), [R\&D](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_and_development), [software](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software), [toys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toy) and [games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Game), [TV](29831.md) and [radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio), and [video games](5363.md).

Maharashtra has an above-average [knowledge industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knowledge_industry) in India, with [Pune Metropolitan Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pune_Metropolitan_Region) being the leading IT hub in the state. Approximately 25% of the top 500 companies in the IT sector are based in Maharashtra.[^cite_note-IT-228] The state accounts for 28% of the software exports of India.[^cite_note-IT-228]

Maharashtra and particularly Mumbai is a prominent location for the Indian entertainment industry, with many films, television series, books, and other media being set there.[^cite_note-ibef-229] Mumbai is the largest centre for film and television production and a third of all Indian films are produced in the state. Multimillion-dollar [Bollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bollywood) productions, with the most expensive costing up to ₹1.5 billion (US\$16 million), are filmed there.[^cite_note-time_corliss-230] [Marathi films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_film) used to be previously made primarily in [Kolhapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolhapur), but now are produced in Mumbai.[^cite_note-231]

The state houses important financial institutions such as the [Reserve Bank of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserve_Bank_of_India), the Bombay Stock Exchange, the [National Stock Exchange of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Stock_Exchange_of_India), the [SEBI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Securities_and_Exchange_Board_of_India) and the corporate [headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_companies_headquartered_in_Mumbai) of numerous [Indian companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_companies) and [multinational corporations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multinational_corporations). It is also home to some of India's premier scientific and nuclear institutes like [BARC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhabha_Atomic_Research_Centre), [NPCL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_Power_Corporation_of_India), [IREL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Rare_Earths_Limited), [TIFR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Institute_of_Fundamental_Research), [AERB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_Energy_Regulatory_Board), [AECI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_Energy_Commission_of_India), and the [Department of Atomic Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Atomic_Energy_\(India\)).[^cite_note-investment-225]

refer caption

Freshly grown [sugarcane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane). Agriculture is the second leading occupation in Maharashtra.

With more than half the population being rural, agriculture and allied industries play an important role in the states's economy and source of income for the rural population.[^cite_note-Kalamkar2011-232] The agriculture and allied activities sector contributes 12.9% to the state's income. Staples such as [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) and [millet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millet) are the main [monsoon](57630.md) crops. Important [cash crops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cash_crop) include [sugarcane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane), [cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotton), oilseeds, tobacco, fruit, vegetables, and spices such as [turmeric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turmeric).[^cite_note-farmer-100]

Animal husbandry is an important agriculture-related activity. The State's share in the livestock and poultry population in India is about 7% and 10%, respectively. Maharashtra was a pioneer in the development of [Agricultural Cooperative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_Cooperative) Societies after independence. It was an integral part of the then Governing [Congress](149333.md) party's vision of 'rural development with local initiative'. A 'special' status was accorded to the [sugar](27712.md) cooperatives and the government assumed the role of a mentor by acting as a stakeholder, guarantor, and regulator,[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234][^cite_note-helsinki-235] Apart from sugar, cooperatives play a crucial role in dairy,[^cite_note-236] cotton, and fertiliser industries.

The banking sector comprises [scheduled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scheduled_banks) and non-scheduled banks.[^cite_note-IT-228] Scheduled banks are of two types, commercial and cooperative. Scheduled Commercial Banks (SCBs) in India are classified into five types: [State Bank of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Bank_of_India) and its associates, nationalised banks, [private sector banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private-sector_banks_in_India), [Regional Rural Banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Rural_Bank), and others (foreign banks). In 2012, there were 9,053 banking offices in the state, of which about 26% were in rural and 54% were in urban areas. Maharashtra has a [microfinance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microfinance) system, which refers to small-scale financial services extended to the poor in both rural and urban areas. It covers a variety of financial instruments, such as lending, savings, life insurance, and crop insurance.[^cite_note-237] The three largest urban [cooperative banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooperative_banking) in India are all based in Maharashtra.[^cite_note-238]

## Transport

Main article: [Transport in Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Maharashtra)

See also: [List of airports in Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Maharashtra)

Landscaped four-lane road over rolling terrain

[Mumbai–Nashik Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai%E2%80%93Nashik_Expressway)&#x20;



[Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhatrapati_Shivaji_Maharaj_International_Airport) (Mumbai)



[Nagpur Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagpur_Metro)



A container ship at [Jawaharlal Nehru Port Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru_Port_Trust)

The state has a large, multi-modal transportation system with the largest road network in India.[^cite_note-239] In 2011, the total length of surface road in Maharashtra was 267,452 km (166,187 mi);[^cite_note-Highwaylength-240] national highways accounted for 4,176 km (2,595 mi),[^cite_note-241] and state highways 3,700 km (2,300 mi).[^cite_note-Highwaylength-240] The [Maharashtra State Road Transport Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_State_Road_Transport_Corporation) (MSRTC) provides economical and reliable passenger road transport service in the public sector.[^cite_note-msrtc.gov.in-242] These buses, popularly called ST (State Transport), are the preferred mode of transport for much of the populace. Hired forms of transport include metered taxis and [auto-rickshaws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auto_rickshaws), which often ply specific routes in cities.[^cite_note-msrtc.gov.in-242]

Other district roads and village roads provide villages, accessibility to meet their social needs as well as the means to transport agricultural produce from villages to nearby markets. Major district roads provide a secondary function of linking between main roads and rural roads. Approximately 98% of villages are connected either via the highways or modern roads in Maharashtra. Average speed on state highways varies between 50–60 km/h (31–37 mph) due to the heavy presence of vehicles; in villages and towns, speeds are as low as 25–30 km/h (16–19 mph).[^cite_note-243]

In April 1853, the first passenger train in India ran from Mumbai to Thane.[^cite_note-244] Rail transportation is run by the [Central Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Railway_zone), [Western Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Railway_zone), [South Central Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Central_Railway_zone), [Konkan Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkan_Railway) and [South East Central Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_East_Central_Railway_zone) zones of the [Indian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Railways) with the first two zones being headquartered in Mumbai, at [Chhatrapati Shivaji Terminus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhatrapati_Shivaji_Terminus) (CSMT) and [Churchgate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Churchgate) respectively. The [Konkan Railway Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkan_Railway_Corporation) is headquartered in [Navi Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navi_Mumbai).[^cite_note-western-245][^cite_note-central-246] The [Mumbai Rajdhani Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Rajdhani_Express), the fastest [Rajdhani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajdhani_Express) train, connects the Indian capital of [New Delhi](51585.md) to Mumbai.[^cite_note-rajdhani-247] [Thane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thane_railway_station) and [CSMT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhatrapati_Shivaji_Terminus) are among the busiest railway stations in India,[^cite_note-248] the latter serving as a terminal for both long-distance trains and commuter trains of the Mumbai Suburban Railway.

The two principal seaports, [Mumbai Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Port_Trust) and [Jawaharlal Nehru Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru_Port), which is also in the Mumbai region, are under the control and supervision of the government of India.[^cite_note-249] There are around 48 minor ports in Maharashtra.[^cite_note-250] Most of these handle passenger traffic and have a limited capacity. None of the major rivers in Maharashtra are navigable, and thus river transport does not exist in the state.

[Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhatrapati_Shivaji_Maharaj_International_Airport) (formerly Bombay International Airport), is the state's largest airport. The four other international airports are [Pune International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pune_International_Airport), [Dr. Babasaheb Ambedkar International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Babasaheb_Ambedkar_International_Airport) at [Nagpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagpur), [Nashik Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashik_Airport) and [Shirdi Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirdi_Airport). [Aurangabad Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurangabad_Airport), [Kolhapur Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolhapur_Airport), [Jalgaon Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalgaon_Airport), and [Nanded Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanded_Airport) are domestic airports in the state. Most of the State's airfields are operated by the [Airports Authority of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airports_Authority_of_India) (AAI).[^cite_note-TOI-251]

[Reliance Airport Developers (RADPL)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reliance_Infrastructure#Reliance_Airport_Project), operates five non-metro airports at [Latur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latur_Airport), [Nanded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanded_Airport), [Baramati](30640828.md), [Osmanabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osmanabad_Airport) and [Yavatmal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yavatmal_airport) on a 95-year lease.[^cite_note-TOI-251] The [Maharashtra Airport Development Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Airport_Development_Company) (MADC) was set up in 2002 to take up development of airports in the state that are not under the AAI or the [Maharashtra Industrial Development Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Industrial_Development_Corporation) (MIDC). MADC is playing the lead role in the planning and implementation of the [Multi-modal International Cargo Hub and Airport at Nagpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-modal_International_Cargo_Hub_and_Airport_at_Nagpur) (MIHAN) project.[^cite_note-MADC-252]

Additional smaller airports include [Akola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akola_Airport), [Amravati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amravati_Airport), [Chandrapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chandrapur_Airport), [Ratnagiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratnagiri_Airport), and [Solapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solapur_Airport).[^cite_note-smaller-253] [Maharashtra Metro Rail Corporation Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Metro_Rail_Corporation_Limited) (Maha Metro), which is a [joint Venture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Venture) establishment of [Government of India](553883.md) & [Government of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Maharashtra), is headquartered at [Nagpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagpur), India. Maha Metro is responsible for the implementation of all Maharashtra state metro projects, except the [Mumbai Metropolitan Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Metropolitan_Region). [Mumbai Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Metro) has been operational since 8 June 2014.[^cite_note-254]

## Education

See also: [Education in Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Maharashtra) and [List of higher education institutions in Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_higher_education_institutions_in_Maharashtra)



Students at a state-run primary school in [Raigad district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raigad_district)

The 2011 census showed that state literacy rates for males and females were around 88.38% and 75.87% respectively.[^cite_note-255]

Regions that comprise the present day state of Maharashtra have been known for their pioneering role in the development of the modern education system in India. Scottish missionary [John Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Wilson_\(missionary\)), American Marathi mission, Indian nationalists such as [Vasudev Balwant Phadke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasudev_Balwant_Phadke) and [Bal Gangadhar Tilak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bal_Gangadhar_Tilak), social reformers such as [Jyotirao Phule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyotirao_Phule), [Dhondo Keshav Karve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhondo_Keshav_Karve) and [Bhaurao Patil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhaurao_Patil) played a leading role in the setting up of modern schools and colleges during the British colonial era.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257][^cite_note-258][^cite_note-Smith2012-259]

The forerunner of [Deccan College Post-Graduate and Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_College_Post-Graduate_and_Research_Institute) was established in 1821. The [Shreemati Nathibai Damodar Thackersey Women's University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shreemati_Nathibai_Damodar_Thackersey_Women's_University), the oldest women's liberal arts college in South Asia, started its journey in 1916. [College of Engineering Pune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Engineering_Pune), established in 1854, is the third oldest college in Asia.[^cite_note-260] [Government Polytechnic Nagpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Polytechnic_Nagpur), established in 1914, is one of the oldest polytechnics in India.[^cite_note-261] Most of the private colleges including religious and special-purpose institutions were set up in the last thirty years after the State Government of [Vasantdada Patil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasantdada_Patil) liberalised the Education Sector in 1982.[^cite_note-262]

### Primary and secondary level education

Schools in the state are either managed by the government or by private trusts, including religious institutions. The medium of instruction in most of the schools is mainly Marathi, English, or Hindi, though [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) is also used. The secondary schools are affiliated with the [Council for the Indian School Certificate Examinations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_for_the_Indian_School_Certificate_Examinations) (CISCE), the [Central Board for Secondary Education (CBSE)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Board_of_Secondary_Education), the [National Institute of Open School (NIOS)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Open_Schooling), and the [Maharashtra State Board of Secondary and Higher Secondary Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_State_Board_of_Secondary_and_Higher_Secondary_Education).

Under the [10+2+3 plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/10+2+3_plan), after completing secondary school, students typically enroll for two years in a [junior college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_college), also known as pre-university, or in schools with a higher secondary facility affiliated with the [Maharashtra State Board of Secondary and Higher Secondary Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_State_Board_of_Secondary_and_Higher_Secondary_Education) or any central board. Students choose from one of three streams, namely [liberal arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_arts), commerce, or science. Upon completing the required coursework, students may enrol in general or professional degree programs.

### Tertiary education



Founded in 1887, [Veermata Jijabai Technological Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veermata_Jijabai_Technological_Institute) (VJTI) is one of the oldest engineering colleges in Asia.



Situated in Pune, [Armed Forces Medical College, Pune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_Forces_Medical_College,_Pune) was established in 1948 after the Indian independence.

Maharashtra has 24 universities with a turnout of 160,000 Graduates every year.[^cite_note-educational_institute-263][^cite_note-universities-264] Established during the rule of [East India company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company_rule) in 1857 as Bombay University, The [University of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Mumbai), is the largest university in the world in terms of the number of graduates.[^cite_note-265] It has 141 affiliated colleges.[^cite_note-colleges-266] According to a report published by The Times Education magazine, 5 to 7 Maharashtra colleges and universities are ranked among the top 20 in India.[^cite_note-top_colleges-267][^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269]

Maharashtra is also home to notable autonomous institutes as [Indian Institute of Technology Bombay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Technology_Bombay), [Indian Institute of Information Technology Pune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Information_Technology,_Pune), [College of Engineering Pune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Engineering_Pune) (CoEP), [Dr. Babasaheb Ambedkar Technological University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Babasaheb_Ambedkar_Technological_University), [Institute of Chemical Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Chemical_Technology), [Homi Bhabha National Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homi_Bhabha_National_Institute), [Walchand College of Engineering, Sangli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walchand_College_of_Engineering,_Sangli), and [Veermata Jijabai Technological Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veermata_Jijabai_Technological_Institute) (VJTI), [Sardar Patel College of Engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sardar_Patel_College_of_Engineering) (SPCE).[^cite_note-autonomous_colleges-270]

Most of these autonomous institutes are ranked the highest in India and have very competitive entry requirements. The [University of Pune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pune) (now [Savitribai Phule Pune University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savitribai_Phule_Pune_University)), the [National Defence Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Defence_Academy_\(India\)), [Film and Television Institute of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_and_Television_Institute_of_India), [Armed Forces Medical College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_Forces_Medical_College,_Pune), and [National Chemical Laboratory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Chemical_Laboratory) were established in Pune soon after the Indian independence in 1947. Mumbai has an [IIT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institutes_of_Technology), an [IIM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institutes_of_Management) and also [National Institute of Industrial Engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Industrial_Engineering) and Nagpur has [IIM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institutes_of_Management) and [AIIMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIIMS). Other notable institutes in the state are: [Maharashtra National Law University, Nagpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_National_Law_University,_Nagpur) (MNLUN), [Maharashtra National Law University, Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_National_Law_University,_Mumbai) (MNLUM), [Maharashtra National Law University, Aurangabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_National_Law_University,_Aurangabad) (MNLUA), [Government Law College, Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Law_College,_Mumbai) (GLC), [ILS Law College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ILS_Law_College), and [Symbiosis Law School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symbiosis_Law_School) (SLS)



Panjabrao Deshmukh Krishi Vidyapeeth (Agricultural University) at Akola

Agricultural universities include [Vasantrao Naik Marathwada Agricultural University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasantrao_Naik_Marathwada_Agricultural_University), [Mahatma Phule Krishi Vidyapeeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahatma_Phule_Krishi_Vidyapeeth), [Dr. Panjabrao Deshmukh Krishi Vidyapeeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Panjabrao_Deshmukh_Krishi_Vidyapeeth), and [Dr. Balasaheb Sawant Konkan Krishi Vidyapeeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Balasaheb_Sawant_Konkan_Krishi_Vidyapeeth),[^cite_note-mcaer-271] Regional universities viz. [Sant Gadge Baba Amravati University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sant_Gadge_Baba_Amravati_University), [Dr. Babasaheb Ambedkar Marathwada University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Babasaheb_Ambedkar_Marathwada_University), [North Maharashtra University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Maharashtra_University), [Shivaji University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shivaji_University), [Solapur University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solapur_University), [Swami Ramanand Teerth Marathwada University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swami_Ramanand_Teerth_Marathwada_University), and [Rashtrasant Tukadoji Maharaj Nagpur University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashtrasant_Tukadoji_Maharaj_Nagpur_University) are established to cover the educational needs at the district levels of the state. [deemed universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deemed_university) are established in Maharashtra, including [Symbiosis International University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symbiosis_International_University), [Tata Institute of Social Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Institute_of_Social_Sciences), and [Tilak Maharashtra University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tilak_Maharashtra_University).[^cite_note-272]

Vocational training in different trades such as construction, plumbing, welding, automobile mechanics is offered by post-secondary school [industrial training institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_training_institute) (ITIs).[^cite_note-273] Local [community colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_colleges) also exist with generally more open admission policies, shorter academic programs, and lower tuition.[^cite_note-274]

## Infrastructure

### Healthcare



A Primary Healthcare centre in the village of Amboli in Pune district

Health indicators of Maharashtra show that they have attained relatively high growth against a background of high per capita income (PCI).[^cite_note-PCI-275] In 2011, the health care system in Maharashtra consisted of 363 rural government hospitals,[^cite_note-Hospital_Services-276] 23 district hospitals (with 7,561 beds), 4 general hospitals (with 714 beds) mostly under the Maharashtra [Ministry of Health and Family Welfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_and_Family_Welfare), and 380 private medical establishments; these establishments provide the state with more than 30,000 hospital beds.[^cite_note-women-277] It is the first state in India to have nine women's hospitals serving 1,365 beds.[^cite_note-women-277] The state also has a significant number of medical practitioners who hold the [Bachelor of Ayurveda, Medicine and Surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelor_of_Ayurveda,_Medicine_and_Surgery) qualifications. These practitioners primarily use the traditional Indian therapy of [Ayurveda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayurveda), nevertheless, modern western medicine is used as well.[^cite_note-278]

In Maharashtra and the rest of India, Primary Health Centre (PHC) is part of the government-funded public health system and is the most basic unit of the healthcare system. They are essentially single-physician clinics usually with facilities for minor surgeries, too.[^cite_note-PublicHealthStandards-279] Maharashtra has a life expectancy at birth of 67.2 years in 2011, ranking it [third](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_by_life_expectancy_at_birth) among 29 [Indian states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_and_territories_of_India).[^cite_note-health-280] The [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) of the state is 1.9.[^cite_note-Fertility-281] The [Infant mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality) rate is 28 and the [maternal mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maternal_death) ratio is 104 (2012–2013), which are lower than the national averages.[^cite_note-mortality-282][^cite_note-Infant_mortality-283] [Public health services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_health_system_in_India) are governed by the [Ministry of Health and Family Welfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_and_Family_Welfare) (MoHFW), through various departments. The Ministry is divided into two departments: the Public Health Department, which includes family welfare and medical relief, and the Department of Medical Education and Drugs.[^cite_note-public-284][^cite_note-Public_health-285]

Health insurance includes any program that helps pay for medical expenses, through privately purchased insurance, [social insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_insurance), or a [social welfare program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_welfare_provision) funded by the government.[^cite_note-Statistics-286] In a more technical sense, the term is used to describe any form of insurance that protects against the costs of medical services.[^cite_note-diagnostic-287] This usage includes private insurance and social insurance programs such as National Health Mission, which pools resources and spreads the financial risk associated with major medical expenses across the entire population to protect everyone, as well as social welfare programs such as [National Rural Health Mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rural_Health_Mission) (NRHM) and the Health Insurance Program, which assist people who cannot afford health coverage.[^cite_note-Statistics-286][^cite_note-diagnostic-287][^cite_note-status-288]

Maharashtra was ranked fifth in the Fiscal Health Index (FHI) 2025, with a score of 50.3.[^cite_note-289]

### Energy

Current functioning units of Chandrapur Super Thermal Power Station

[Chandrapur Super Thermal Power Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chandrapur_Super_Thermal_Power_Station), the state's power production source

Its population makes Maharashtra one of India's largest energy users.[^cite_note-consumes-290][^cite_note-Thermal-291] Conservation mandates, mild weather in the largest population centres, and strong environmental movements have kept its per capita energy use to one of the smallest of any Indian state.[^cite_note-Regulatory-292] The high electricity demand of the state constitutes 13% of the total installed electricity generation capacity in India, which is derived mainly from fossil fuels such as coal and natural gas.[^cite_note-293] [Mahavitaran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahavitaran) is responsible for the distribution of electricity throughout the state by buying power from Mahanirmiti, captive power plants, other state electricity boards, and private sector power generation companies.[^cite_note-Regulatory-292]

As of 2012, Maharashtra was the largest power generating state in India, with an installed electricity generation capacity of 26,838 MW.[^cite_note-Thermal-291] The state forms a major constituent of the western grid of India, which now comes under the North, East, West and North Eastern (NEWNE) grids of India.[^cite_note-consumes-290] Maharashtra Power Generation Company (MAHAGENCO) operates [thermal power plants](2549543.md).[^cite_note-294] In addition to the state government-owned power generation plants, there are privately owned power generation plants that transmit power through the [Maharashtra State Electricity Transmission Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_State_Electricity_Transmission_Company), which is responsible for the transmission of electricity in the state.[^cite_note-power_supply-295]

### Environmental protection and sustainability

[Maharashtra Pollution Control Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Pollution_Control_Board) (MPCB) is established and responsible for implementing various environmental legislations in the state principally including the Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974, Air (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1981, Water (Cess) Act, 1977 and some of the provisions under Environmental (Protection) Act, 1986 and the rules framed there under it including, Biomedical Waste (M\&H) Rules, 1998, Hazardous Waste (M\&H) Rules, 2000, and Municipal Solid Waste Rules, 2000. MPCB is functioning under the administrative control of the Environment Department of the [Government of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Maharashtra).[^cite_note-MPCB-296] The Maharashtra Plastic and Thermocol Products ban became effective as law on 23 June 2018, subjecting plastic users to fines and potential imprisonment for repeat offenders.[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298]

## Culture

Main articles: [Culture of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Maharashtra) and [List of State Protected Monuments in Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_State_Protected_Monuments_in_Maharashtra)



Maharashtrian vegetarian Thali



Misal Paav, a popular dish from Maharashtra

### Cuisine

Main article: [Maharashtrian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtrian_cuisine)

Maharashtrian cuisine includes a variety of dishes ranging from mild to very spicy ones. Wheat, rice, *[jowar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jowar)*, *[bajri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bajri)*, vegetables, lentils and fruit form [staple food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staple_food) of the Maharashtrian diet. Some of the popular traditional dishes include *[puran poli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puran_poli)*, *ukdiche [modak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modak)*, *[Thalipeeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thalipeeth).*[^cite_note-Khatau2004-299] Street food items like *[Batata wada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batata_wada),* *[Misal Pav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misal_Pav)*, *[Pav Bhaji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pav_Bhaji)* an&#x64;*&#x20;[Vada pav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vada_pav)* are very popular among the locals and are usually sold on stalls and in small hotels.[^cite_note-cuisine-300]

Meals (mainly lunch and dinner) are served on a plate called [thali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thali). Each food item served on the *thali* is arranged in a specific way. All non-vegetarian and vegetarian dishes are eaten with boiled rice, [chapatis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapati) or with [bhakris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhakri), made of jowar, bajra or rice flours. A typical vegetarian thali is made of [chapati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapati) or [bhakri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhakri) (Indian flat bread), [dal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dal), rice (varan bhaat), amti, *[bhaji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhaji) or [usal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Usal)*, [chutney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chutney), *koshimbir* (salad) and [buttermilk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buttermilk) or *Sol kadhi*.[^cite_note-cuisine-300][^cite_note-Maharashtra_Recipes-301]

A *bhaji* is a vegetable dish made of a particular vegetable or combination of vegetables. *Aamti* is variant of the curry, typically consisting of a lentil (*tur*) stock, flavoured with goda masala and sometimes with tamarind or amshul, and [jaggery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaggery) (*gul*).[^cite_note-cuisine-300][^cite_note-Maharashtra_Recipes-301] *Varan* is nothing but plain [dal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dal), a common Indian lentil stew. More or less, most of the dishes use coconut, onion, garlic, ginger, red chili powder, green chilies, and mustard though some section of the population traditionally avoid onion and garlics.[^cite_note-Annathali-302][^cite_note-cuisine-300]

Maharashtrian cuisine varies with the regions. Traditional [Malvani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malvani_Konkani) (Konkani), Kolhapuri, and Varhadhi dishes are examples of well known regional cuisines.[^cite_note-Annathali-302] Kolhapur is famous for Tambda Pandhra rassa, a dish made of either chicken or mutton.[^cite_note-303] Rice and seafood are the staple foods of the coastal Konkani people. Among seafood, the most popular is a fish variety called the [Bombay duck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_duck) (also known as bombil in Marathi).

### Attire



A woman wearing traditional Maharashtrian Nauvari sari (a nine-yard sari)

Traditionally, Marathi women commonly wore the [sari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sari), often distinctly designed according to local cultural customs.[^cite_note-Costumes-304] Most middle-aged and young women in urban Maharashtra dress in western outfits such as skirts and trousers or [shalwar kameez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shalwar_kameez) with the traditionally *[nauvari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kasta_sari#Nauvari)* or nine-yard [lugade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kasta_sari),[^cite_note-305] disappearing from the markets due to a lack of demand.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKher2003-306] Older women wear the five-yard sari. In urban areas, the five-yard sari, especially the [Paithani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paithani), is worn by younger women for special occasions such as marriages and religious ceremonies.[^cite_note-307]

Among men, western dressing has greater acceptance. Men also wear traditional costumes such as the [dhoti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhoti), and [pheta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pheta_\(turban\))[^cite_note-308] on cultural occasions. The [Gandhi cap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gandhi_cap) is the popular headgear among older men in rural Maharashtra.[^cite_note-Costumes-304][^cite_note-309][^cite_note-tradition-310] Women wear traditional jewellery derived from Maratha and Peshwa dynasties. [Kolhapuri saaj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolhapuri_saaj), a special type of necklace, is also worn by Marathi women.[^cite_note-Costumes-304] In urban areas, western attire is dominant among women and men.[^cite_note-tradition-310]

### Music

Maharashtra and Maharashtrian artists have been influential in preserving and developing [Hindustani classical music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustani_classical_music) for more than a century. Notable practitioners of [Kirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirana_gharana) or [Gwalior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwalior_gharana) style called Maharashtra their home. The [Sawai Gandharva Bhimsen Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sawai_Gandharva_Bhimsen_Festival) in Pune started by [Bhimsen Joshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhimsen_Joshi) in the 1950s is considered the most prestigious Hindustani music festival in India, if not one of the largest.[^cite_note-311]

Cities like Kolhapur and Pune have been playing a major role in the preservation of music like [Bhavageet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhavageet) and [Natya Sangeet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natya_Sangeet), which are inherited from Indian classical music. The biggest form of Indian [popular music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_music) is songs from films produced in Mumbai. Film music, in 2009 made up 72% of the music sales in India.[^cite_note-312] Many the influential music composers and singers have called Mumbai their home.

In recent decades, the music scene in Maharashtra, and particularly in Mumbai has seen a growth of newer music forms such as rap.[^cite_note-313] The city also holds festivals in western music genres such as [blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahindra_Blues_Festival).[^cite_note-314] In 2006, the [Symphony Orchestra of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symphony_Orchestra_of_India) was founded, housed at the [NCPA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Centre_for_the_Performing_Arts_\(India\)) in Mumbai. It is today the only professional symphony orchestra in India and presents two concert seasons per year, with world-renowned conductors and soloists.

Maharashtra has a long and rich tradition of folk music. Some of the most common forms of folk music in practice are Bhajan, Bharud, Kirtan, Gondhal,[^cite_note-315] and [Koli Geet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koli_Geet).[^cite_note-316]

### Dance



A Lavani performance

Marathi dance forms draw from folk traditions. [Lavani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lavani) is popular form of dance in the state. The Bhajan, Kirtan and [Abhangas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abhang) of the [Warkari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warkari) sect (Vaishanav Devotees) have a long history and are part of their daily rituals.[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318] [Koli dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koli_dance?action=edit\&redlink=1) (called 'Koligeete') is among the most popular dances of Maharashtra. As the name suggests, it is related to the fisher folk of Maharashtra, who are called [Koli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koli_people). Popular for their unique identity and liveliness, their dances represent their occupation. This type of dance is represented by both men and women. While dancing, they are divided into groups of two. These fishermen display the movements of waves and casting of the nets during their koli dance performances.[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320]

### Theatre

Main article: [Marathi theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_theatre)



Playwright [Vijay Tendulkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijay_Tendulkar)

Modern Theatre in Maharashtra can trace its origins to the British colonial era in the middle of the 19th century. It is modelled mainly after the western tradition but also includes forms like [Sangeet Natak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangeet_Natak) (musical drama). In recent decades, Marathi [Tamasha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamasha) has also been incorporated in some experimental plays.[^cite_note-321] The repertoire of Marathi theatre ranges from humorous social plays, [farces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farce), historical plays, and musical, to experimental plays and serious drama. Marathi Playwrights such as [Vijay Tendulkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijay_Tendulkar), [Purushottam Laxman Deshpande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purushottam_Laxman_Deshpande), [Mahesh Elkunchwar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahesh_Elkunchwar), [Ratnakar Matkari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratnakar_Matkari), and [Satish Alekar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satish_Alekar) have influenced theatre throughout India.[^cite_note-322] Besides Marathi theatre, Maharashtra and particularly, Mumbai, has had a long tradition of theatre in other languages such as Gujarati, Hindi, and English.[^cite_note-323]

The [National Centre for the Performing Arts (NCP)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Centre_for_the_Performing_Arts_\(India\)) is a multi-venue, multi-purpose cultural centre in Mumbai which hosts events in music, dance, theatre, film, literature, and photography from India as well other places. It also presents new and innovative work in the performing arts field.

### Literature

Main article: [Marathi literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_literature)



[P L Deshpande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purushottam_Laxman_Deshpande) (at centre), one of the most popular authors in Marathi language

Maharashtra's regional literature is about the lives and circumstances of [Marathi people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_people) in specific parts of the state. The Marathi language, which boasts a rich literary heritage, is written in the [Devanagari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devanagari) script.[^cite_note-324] The earliest instance of Marathi literature is [Dnyaneshwari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dnyaneshwari), a commentary on the Bhagavad Gita by 13th-century [Bhakti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhakti) Saint [Dnyaneshwar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dnyaneshwar) and devotional poems called [abhangs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abhang) by his contemporaries such as [Namdev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namdev), and [Gora Kumbhar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gora_Kumbhar). Devotional literature from the [Early modern period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_modern_period) includes compositions in praise of the God [Pandurang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandurang) by [Bhakti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhakti) saints such as [Tukaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tukaram), [Eknath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eknath), and [Rama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rama) by [Ramdas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samarth_Ramdas) respectively.[^cite_note-Singh-325][^cite_note-326]

19th century [Marathi literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_literature) includes mainly [Polemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polemic) works of social and political activists such as [Balshastri Jambhekar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balshastri_Jambhekar), [Bal Gangadhar Tilak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bal_Gangadhar_Tilak), [Gopal Hari Deshmukh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopal_Hari_Deshmukh), [Mahadev Govind Ranade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahadev_Govind_Ranade), [Jyotirao Phule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyotirao_Phule), and [Vishnushastri Krushnashastri Chiplunkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vishnushastri_Krushnashastri_Chiplunkar). [Keshavasuta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keshavasuta) was a pioneer in modern Marathi poetry. The [Hindutva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindutva) proponent, [Vinayak Damodar Savarkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinayak_Damodar_Savarkar) was a prolific writer. His work in English and Marathi consists of many essays, two novels, poetry, and plays.

Four Marathi writers have been honoured with the [Jnanpith Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jnanpith_Award), India's highest literary award. They include novelists, [Vishnu Sakharam Khandekar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vishnu_Sakharam_Khandekar), and [Bhalchandra Nemade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhalchandra_Nemade), [Vishnu Vaman Shirwadkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kusumagraj) (Kusumagraj) and [Vinda Karandikar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinda_Karandikar). The last two were known for their poetry as well.[^cite_note-327] Other notable writers from the early and mid 20th century include playwright [Ram Ganesh Gadkari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ram_Ganesh_Gadkari), novelist [Hari Narayan Apte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hari_Narayan_Apte), poet, and novelist [B. S. Mardhekar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B._S._Mardhekar), [Pandurang Sadashiv Sane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandurang_Sadashiv_Sane), [Vyankatesh Madgulkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vyankatesh_Madgulkar), [Pralhad Keshav Atre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pralhad_Keshav_Atre), [Chintamani Tryambak Khanolkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chintamani_Tryambak_Khanolkar), and [Lakshman Shastri Joshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakshman_Shastri_Joshi). [Vishwas Patil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vishwas_Patil), [Ranjit Desai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranjit_Desai), and [Shivaji Sawant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shivaji_Sawant) are known for novels based on Maratha history. P. L. Deshpande gained popularity in the period after independence for depicting the urban middle class society. His work includes humour, travelogues, plays, and biographies.[^cite_note-328] [Narayan Gangaram Surve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narayan_Gangaram_Surve), [Shanta Shelke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanta_Shelke), [Durga Bhagwat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durga_Bhagwat), [Suresh Bhat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suresh_Bhat), and [Narendra Jadhav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narendra_Jadhav) are some of the more recent authors.

[Dalit literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalit_literature) originally emerged in the Marathi language as a literary response to the everyday oppressions of caste in mid-twentieth-century independent India, critiquing caste practices by experimenting with various literary forms.[^cite_note-329] In 1958, the term "Dalit literature" was used for the first conference of *Maharashtra Dalit Sahitya Sangha* (Maharashtra Dalit Literature Society) in Mumbai.[^cite_note-330]

Maharashtra, and particularly the cities in the state such as Mumbai and Pune are diverse with different languages being spoken. Mumbai is called home by writers in English such as [Rohinton Mistry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohinton_Mistry), [Shobha De](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shobha_De), and [Salman Rushdie](28722.md). Their novels are set with Mumbai as the backdrop.[^cite_note-331] Many eminent [Urdu poets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Sahitya_Akademi_Award_winners_for_Urdu) such as [Kaifi Azmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaifi_Azmi), [Jan Nisar Akhtar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Nisar_Akhtar), [Gulzar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulzar), and [Javed Akhtar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javed_Akhtar) have been residents of Mumbai.

### Cinema

Main articles: [Bollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bollywood) and [Marathi cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_cinema)



[Amitabh Bachchan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amitabh_Bachchan) of [Bollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bollywood), called "the biggest film star in the world".

[^cite_note-332]

[^cite_note-333]

[^cite_note-334]

Maharashtra is a prominent location for the Indian entertainment industry, with enormous films, television series, books, and other media production companies being set there.[^cite_note-ibef-229] Mumbai has numerous film production studios and facilities to produce films.[^cite_note-335] Mainstream [Hindi films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindi_films) are popular in Maharashtra, especially in urban areas. Mumbai is the largest centre for film and television production and a third of all Indian films are produced in the state. Multimillion-dollar [Bollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bollywood) productions, with the most expensive costing up to ₹1.5 billion (US\$16 million), are filmed there.[^cite_note-time_corliss-230]

The first Indian feature-length film, [Raja Harishchandra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raja_Harishchandra), was made in Maharashtra by [Dadasaheb Phalke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dadasaheb_Phalke) in 1913.[^cite_note-336] Phalke is widely considered the father of [Indian cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_India).[^cite_note-337] The [Dadasaheb Phalke Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dadasaheb_Phalke_Award) is India's highest award in cinema, given annually by the [Government of India](553883.md) for lifetime contribution to [Indian cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_cinema).[^cite_note-dadasahebdff-338]

The [Marathi film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_film) industry, initially located in Kolhapur, has spread throughout Mumbai. Well known for its [art films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_films), the early Marathi film industry included acclaimed directors such as Dadasaheb Phalke, [V. Shantaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V._Shantaram), [Raja Thakur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raja_Thakur), [Bhalji Pendharkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhalji_Pendharkar), [Pralhad Keshav Atre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pralhad_Keshav_Atre), [Baburao Painter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baburao_Painter), and [Dada Kondke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dada_Kondke). Some of the directors who made acclaimed films in Marathi are [Jabbar Patel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jabbar_Patel), [Mahesh Manjrekar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahesh_Manjrekar), [Amol Palekar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amol_Palekar), and [Sanjay Surkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanjay_Surkar).

[Durga Khote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durga_Khote) was one of the first women from respectable families to enter the film industry, thus breaking a social taboo.[^cite_note-339] [Lalita Pawar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lalita_Pawar), [Sulabha Deshpande, and](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulabha_Deshpande) [Usha Kiran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Usha_Kiran) featured in Hindi and Marathi movies. In 70s and 80s, [Smita Patil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smita_Patil), [Ranjana Deshmukh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranjana_Deshmukh), [Reema Lagoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reema_Lagoo) featured in both art and mainstream movies in Hindi and Marathi. [Rohini Hattangadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohini_Hattangadi) starred in a number of acclaimed movies, and is the only Indian actress to win the [BAFTA Award for Best Actress in a Supporting Role](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BAFTA_Award_for_Best_Actress_in_a_Supporting_Role) for her performance as [Kasturba Gandhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kasturba_Gandhi) in *[Gandhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gandhi_\(film\))* (1982).[^cite_note-340] [Bhanu Athaiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhanu_Athaiya) was the first Indian to win an Oscar in [Best Costume Design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Costume_Design) category for *[Gandhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gandhi_\(film\))* (1982).[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342] In 90s and 2000s, [Urmila Matondkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urmila_Matondkar) and [Madhuri Dixit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madhuri_Dixit) starred in critically acclaimed and high grossing films in Hindi and Marathi.

In earliest days of Marathi cinema, [Suryakant Mandhare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suryakant_Mandhare) was a leading star.[^cite_note-343] In later years, [Shriram Lagoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shriram_Lagoo), [Nilu Phule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilu_Phule), [Vikram Gokhale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vikram_Gokhale), [Dilip Prabhavalkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dilip_Prabhavalkar) played character roles in theatre, and Hindi and Marathi films. [Ramesh Deo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramesh_Deo) and [Mohan Joshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohan_Joshi) played leading men in Mainstream Marathi movies.[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345] In 70s and 80s, [Sachin Pilgaonkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sachin_Pilgaonkar), [Ashok Saraf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashok_Saraf), [Laxmikant Berde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laxmikant_Berde) and [Mahesh Kothare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahesh_Kothare) created a "comedy film wave" in Marathi Cinema.

## Media



The *Times of India* building in Mumbai

The state is home to more than 200 newspapers and 350 consumer magazines. The publishing industry employs more than 250,000 people.[^cite_note-auditbureau-346] There are number of Marathi news channels in the state namely *[News18 Lokmat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News18_Lokmat), [ABP Majha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABP_Majha)*, *[Jai Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jai_Maharashtra_\(TV_channel\))*, *[Saam TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saam_TV)*, *[Zee 24 Taas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zee_24_Taas)*. As of December 2016, [Sakal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sakal), published in Pune and other major cities, is the largest circulated Marathi Newspaper in Maharashtra.[^cite_note-auditbureau-346] Other major Marathi newspapers include *[Maharashtra Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Times)*, *[Loksatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loksatta)*, *[Nava Kaal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nava_Kaal)*, *[Pudhari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pudhari)*, *[Lokmat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lokmat)* and *[Sakal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sakal)*.[^cite_note-347] *Saptahik Sakal*, *Grihashobhika*, *Lokrajya*, *Lokprabha*, and *Chitralekha* are some of the prominent Marathi magazines.[^cite_note-348]

English language newspapers are confined to urban areas. Some popular among these are *[Daily News & Analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_News_&_Analysis)*, *[The Times of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_India)*, *Hindustan Times*, *[The Indian Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Indian_Express)*, *[Mumbai Mirror](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Mirror)*, *[Asian Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Age)*, *[MiD-DAY](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MiD-DAY)* and *[The Free Press Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Free_Press_Journal)*. Some prominent financial dailies like *[The Economic Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economic_Times)*, *[Mint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mint_\(newspaper\))*, *[Business Standard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Standard)*, and *[The Financial Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Financial_Express_\(India\))* are widely circulated.[^cite_note-349] Many newspapers in other Indian languages such as [Nava Bharat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nava_Bharat) in Hindi, [Udayavani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udayavani) in Kannada, [Mumbai Samachar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Samachar) in Gujarati, and [The Inquilab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Inquilab) in Urdu have Mumbai editions.[^cite_note-350]

The television industry in Maharashtra has been contributing significantly in the state's economy.[^cite_note-351] [Doordarshan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doordarshan), a state-owned television broadcaster runs a channel named [DD Sahyadri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DD_Sahyadri). Numerous [Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_India) and international television channels can be watched in Maharashtra through one of the [Pay TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pay-tv) companies or the local cable television provider. The four major Indian [broadcast networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadcast_network) are all headquartered in Maharashtra: [The Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_Group), [Disney Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Star), [CNN-News18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNN-News18) and [ZEEL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zee_Entertainment_Enterprises). [Multi-system operators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi_system_operator) provide a range of Marathi, Bengali, Nepali, Hindi, English, and international channels via [cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cable_television). There are number of entertainment channels that exclusively streams content in Marathi including [Zee Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zee_Marathi), Zee Yuva, [Colors Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colors_Marathi), [Star Pravah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Pravah), [Sony Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sony_Marathi), and [Fakt Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fakt_Marathi).

[All India Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Radio) airs its content through multiple channels including *Air Marathi*, *FM Gold*, and *FM Rainbow*. Air India's commercially successful air channel *[Vividh Bharti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vividh_Bharti)* is headquartered in Mumbai. Private radio stations like *[Big 92.7 FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_92.7_FM)*, *[Radio Mirchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Mirchi)*, *[Red FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_FM_93.5),* *Vasundhara Vahini*, *Radio Dhamaal 24*, and *My FM* air in all major cities.[^cite_note-Radio-352] [Airtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharti_Airtel), [BSNL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BSNL), [Jio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jio), and [VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Idea) networks provide cellular services and have covered most of the Maharashtra. Broadband internet is available in most of the towns, villages, and cities, provided by the state-run [MTNL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTNL) and [BSNL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BSNL) and by other private companies.[^cite_note-353]

## Sports

Main article: [Sports in Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Maharashtra)

See also: [Sport of cricket in Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Cricket_in_Maharashtra)



Children playing cricket in a farm outside the village of [Chinawal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinawal) in [Jalgaon district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalgaon_district)

Cricket is the most popular spectator sport in Maharashtra. It is played as an exercise and recreational activity. Maharashtra is represented by three teams in Domestic Cricket namely [Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_cricket_team), [Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_cricket_team) and [Vidarbha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidarbha_cricket_team). These teams are governed by [Maharashtra Cricket Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Cricket_Association), [Mumbai Cricket Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Cricket_Association), and [Vidarbha Cricket Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidarbha_Cricket_Association), respectively.[^cite_note-MCA-354][^cite_note-VCA-355][^cite_note-Popular-356][^cite_note-357] Governing body of Cricket in India, BCCI, has its headquarters in [Churchgate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Churchgate), Mumbai. The state also has Indian Premier League (IPL) franchise Mumbai Indians based in Mumbai which plays its home matches at [Wankhede Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wankhede_Stadium). [Brabourne Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brabourne_Stadium), Wankhede Stadium in Mumbai and [New VCA Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidarbha_Cricket_Association_Stadium), Nagpur are international cricket stadiums, which are the venues for international cricket.[^cite_note-Brabourne-358][^cite_note-Wankhede-359]

At [2023 National games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_National_Games_of_India), Maharashtra won highest number of medals among the states and was first in the total medals tally behind [Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Services_Sports_Control_Board). Maharashtra won 228 total medals, of which are 80 gold medals.[^cite_note-360]

[Kabaddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi), [Kho kho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kho_kho), [Kushti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pehlwani), and [Mallakhamba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mallakhamba) are some of the traditional games that are played in the state. [Kusti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pehlwani) and bullock cart races are popular in rural areas of South-west Maharashtra, are organised by villagers during the annual [fairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jatra_\(Maharashtra\)).[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] Amateur wrestling tournaments [Hind Kesari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hind_Kesari), [Maharashtra Kesari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Kesari) are held annually.[^cite_note-kushti-364] Badminton, Volleyball, Tennis are played as a recreational activity in urban areas.[^cite_note-365][^cite_note-366] The beaches of Konkan coast, mountains of the Western Ghats, and the lakes formed by numerous dams in the state facilitate adventure sports such as paragliding, rock climbing, trekking, mountaineering, water sports, and scuba diving and are popular among visitors.[^cite_note-Atithtya-367][^cite_note-368][^cite_note-adventure-369]

India's only world class Tennis championship [Maharashtra Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Open) tennis championship are annually organised in Pune by governing body of tennis in the state, 'Maharashtra Lawn Tennis Association' (MLTA). The tournament is part of [ATP Tour 250](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour_250).[^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372] Mumbai and Pune hold [derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derby_\(horse_race\)) races at the [Mahalaxmi Racecourse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahalaxmi_Racecourse) and [Pune Race Course](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pune_Race_Course) respectively.[^cite_note-race-373][^cite_note-racecourse-374] Various domestic level leagues for field hockey, chess, tennis, and badminton are organised throughout the state.[^cite_note-375] The [Maharashtra football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_football_team) represents the state in the [Santosh Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santosh_Trophy), football tournament. [Pune](164634.md) hosted the [Commonwealth Youth Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Youth_Games) in [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Commonwealth_Youth_Games).

Many national-level football club are based in this state, such as [Mumbai Tigers FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Tigers_FC), [Kenkre FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenkre_FC), [Bengal Mumbai FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_Mumbai_FC), and [Air India FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_India_FC).[^cite_note-clubs-376] Two clubs from the state participate in [Elite Football League of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elite_Football_League_of_India).[^cite_note-indiatimes1-377] [Mumbai Gladiators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Gladiators) and [Pune Marathas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pune_Marathas_\(American_football\)) are teams based in Mumbai and Pune respectively.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] [Puneri Paltan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puneri_Paltan), [U Mumba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U_Mumba), [Pro Kabaddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro_Kabaddi_League), league teams are based in Pune and Mumbai respectively.

## Tourism

See also: [Tourism in Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Maharashtra)

Maharashtra has a number of places that attracts national and international tourists. The most popular or well known are Mumbai city, [Ajanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajanta_Caves), [Ellora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellora_Caves) caves and the Nature reserves in the state.[^cite_note-380] Ajanta–Ellora are the [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Sites). Mumbai, being the biggest and the most cosmopolitan city in India, attracts tourists from all over the world for its many attractions including colonial architecture, beaches, [Bollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bollywood), shopping, and an active nightlife.[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] The city attracts three million foreign and forty million domestic tourists annually. The state wants to increase the numbers by allowing retailers and entertainment venues to be open 24-hours a day, seven days a week.[^cite_note-383]

Pune city, along with [MTDC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTDC), organises many cultural events during Pune festival which coincides with the Hindu [Ganeshotsav festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganesh_Chaturthi).[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] [Hill stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hill_stations) built during the British rule are popular tourist destinations, especially during the summer months. These include [Mahabaleshwar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabaleshwar), Lonavala, and [Matheran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matheran) in Western Maharashtra, and [Chikhaldara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chikhaldara) in the Vidarbha region.[^cite_note-388] The mountainous districts of Western Maharashtra are dotted with the ruins of hundreds of mountain forts from the Deccan Sultanate and the Maratha empire eras. These forts and the surrounding hills are popular among people interested in trekking, hiking, and [heritage tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heritage_tourism) related to Shivaji. Notable forts popular with tourists include [Shivaneri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shivneri_Fort), [Rajgad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajgad_Fort), [Sinhagad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhagad), [Raigad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raigad_Fort), and [Pratapgad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pratapgad_Fort).[^cite_note-389] A group of 11 such forts in Maharashtra along with one fort in Tamil Nadu, officially called the [Maratha Military Landscapes of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha_Military_Landscapes_of_India), were recognised as a UNESCO World Heritage Site in the year 2025.[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391]

A number of temples such as [Trimbakeshwar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trimbakeshwar_Shiva_Temple), [Bhavani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulja_Bhavani_Temple) of [Tuljapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuljapur), [Shani Shingnapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shani_Shingnapur), [Jyotiba Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyotiba_Temple), [Ashtavinayaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashtavinayaka) [Ganapati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganesha) temples, [Lord Pandurang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vithoba) temple at [Pandharpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandharpur) attract a huge number of Hindu devotees every year. [Khandoba temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khandoba_Temple,_Jejuri) of God [Khandoba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khandoba) at [Jejuri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jejuri) in the Pune district attract pilgrims from all over the Maharashtra where worshippers shower each other with *Bhandar* (turmaric powder).[^cite_note-392] [Saibaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sai_Baba_of_Shirdi) temple at [Shirdi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirdi) is visited by an average of 25,000 pilgrims a day and during religious festivals, this number can reach up to 300,000.[^cite_note-393]

The places associated with the [Warkari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varkari) sect such as [Pandharpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandharpur), [Dehu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dehu), and [Alandi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alandi) remain popular throughout the year, and attract huge number of people from all over the state during religious observations.[^cite_note-394] Situated in Nanded, Sikh Gurudwara of [Hazur Sahib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hazur_Sahib_Nanded), also known as Takht Sachkhand Sri Hazur Abchalnagar Sahib, is one of the [five takhts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panj_Takht) in [Sikhism](27964.md). The area around the city of Aurangabad has many ancient and medieval sites including the UNESCO [World Heritage Sites](44940.md) of [Ajanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajanta_Caves) and [Ellora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellora_Caves) caves, the [Daulatabad Fort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daulatabad_Fort), and the [Bibi Ka Maqbara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bibi_Ka_Maqbara).[^cite_note-395]

The Vidarbha region of Maharashtra has numerous nature reserve parks. These include [Melghat Tiger Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melghat_Tiger_Reserve) in Amravati district, [Tadoba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tadoba)-Andhari Tiger Reserve in Chandrapur district,[^cite_note-396] Umred Karhandla Wildlife Sanctuary in Nagpur district, the [Nagzira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagzira) wildlife sanctuary, and [Navegaon National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navegaon_National_Park) (bird sanctuary) of Gondia District.

According to a survey by the government of Maharashtra, in 2009–10, domestic tourists accounted for 98% of the total number of visitors to Maharashtra and the remaining were foreign.[^cite_note-auto2-397] Visitors from the US, UK, Germany, and UAE each form a significant percentage of the foreign tourists.[^cite_note-auto2-397] The state government has established the [Maharashtra Tourism Development Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Tourism_Development_Corporation) (MTDC) for the systematic development and promotion of tourism in the state. MTDC owns and maintains resorts at all key tourist centres.[^cite_note-398]
